# Lab record report

Generated from `record/lab.db` (full export: `record/export.json`).

## Decisions

- **D-001** (PI), cites RS-001: Rung 1 (planned order, v1_support_sort) is PROVISIONALLY passed on T0-T3 dev, limited to no regression vs v0 (0.979 [0.948, 0.992] in both arms, every tier's lower bound >= 0.86). It does NOT count as fully closed: the planner returned the oracle order because the dev scene lists are bottom-up. A scene shuffle option (permute and renumber ids) and shuffled dev scenes T1-dev-07..12, T2-dev-07..10, T3-dev-07..12 are being built. Rung 5 (v5_vlm) proceeds in parallel because it keeps oracle order and is independent. Logged for later: (1) a clean v0 T3 rerun at cbd9b16 (R-009 dirty); (2) cube 90 deg symmetry in the frozen metrics.py, which needs human approval, is conservative and does not change any outcome so far.
  - rationale: The reviewer accepted RS-001 narrowly. Its matched controls, n=48 per tier per arm and CIs all recompute exactly, and held-out was untouched. But the test cannot falsify H-001's real claim (that the planner finds a valid order), because the input order equalled the oracle order. Every failure was placement_error from the roughly 2 cm offset of the scripted placement, not ordering.
  - next: Run v1_support_sort vs v0 on the shuffled dev scenes once they are built and validated, to retest H-001 for real. In parallel, run the rung-5 experiment that experiment_planner is selecting (v5_vlm vs the v0 control on 24 dev scenes). The hard rungs 2/3/4 on T0 are already in engineering.
- **D-002** (PI), cites RS-002: Rung 5 (v5_vlm) passes T0, T2 and T3 dev. It fails T1 (0.67 [0.53-0.78]; effective n = 6 scenes, 4/6). H-002 is refuted for T1. Two causes add together: (a) the VLM quantizes lateral offsets to a 6.5 cm half-grid; (b) the scripted arm has a 1.5-1.9 cm lateral bias that follows the pick side. Most of the 3 cm budget goes to (b). Fix the shared root cause first: closed-loop placement (rung 4, v4_place, already built) should remove (b), and test it on its own before combining it with v5. H-003 (snap hybrid) is deprioritized because RS-002 shows it would not fix T1. A continuous lateral-refinement prompt (method_designer) is the second fix candidate for rung 5. The T0/T2/T3 dev scenes lie on the grid, so they cannot test fine pose. Ask scene_designer for off-grid T2/T3 dev scenes before any rung-5 claim beyond T0.
  - rationale: RS-002 (accepted, narrowly scoped) shows T1 failures are fully explained by the perception rounding and the arm bias adding up. The arm bias appears in every method that uses scripted placement. That makes rung 4 closed-loop placement the highest-leverage fix: it can raise rungs 0, 3 and 5 together.
  - next: v4_place (closed-loop weld place) vs v0 on the T1 dev scenes, then v4_place+VLM on T1. Before that, the analysts close out X-011 (R-017/R-018, shuffled rung-1 test) and X-008 (R-019/R-020, rung-3 grasp). Sumo walking (v2_walk) and MPC placement (v4_mpc) are smoke-tested on T0 in parallel.
- **D-003** (PI), cites RS-003: Rung 1 (planned build order, v1_support_sort) is adopted as the default order stage for all further rungs and combinations, replacing oracle_build_order. H-001 stays supported. No more episodes are spent on rung 1 alone.
  - rationale: RS-003 (accepted by the reviewer): on the shuffled dev scenes, v1 matches v0 exactly under matched scenes and seeds. T1 1.00 [0.93-1.00], T2 0.94 [0.80-0.98], T3 0.98 [0.89-1.00]; effective n is 6/4/6 scenes, so population-level success remains uncertain. The 3 failures are scripted-arm placement errors, not ordering errors. The suspected oracle leak is absent: the engineer audit and the reviewer both confirm support_sort never reads start or oracle_order and sorts by target (z, y), the same key the oracle uses; a regression test was added (tests/test_support_sort.py). Caveat: the shuffled scenes are relabelled copies of dev-01..06, so they add no new structures.
  - next: Pending the X-016 physics result (H-007): decide whether X-014 (v4_place) resumes under default physics or is re-planned under elliptic physics. In parallel: rung-2 v2_walk vs v0 on T0 (planner selecting) and the v4_mpc tipping fix.
- **D-004** (PI), cites RS-005: Rung 3 as built (v3_grasp) is rejected: H-004 refuted. The next rung-3 attempt targets the diagnosed release/retreat defect with v3_grasp_lift (straight vertical lift >= 0.12 m after opening, then retreat; no oracle place correction; default physics), tested under pre-registered H-010. v3_grasp_stack is not run: its 3 cm clearance is too low and its oracle place correction adds an idealization. The T0-dev-02 brick drops in transit are tracked separately and wait for the X-016 physics analysis (elliptic+noslip). T2/T3 work on rung 3 stays stopped until the release is fixed, per H-004's own rule.
  - rationale: RS-005 (accepted by the reviewer): v3_grasp T1 0/48 [0, 0.074] vs v0 48/48 (0/6 vs 6/6 scenes, Fisher p about 0.002); T0 36/48 = 0.75 [0.61, 0.85], and out-of-sample seeds 1-7 30/42 = 0.71. Logs and video show 41/48 T1 failures happen AFTER release: the open jaw retreats sideways along the tool axis and knocks the top block 20-40 cm. T0 failures are 7 brick drops in transit (T0-dev-02) and 5 post-release drags. The reviewer judged the analyst's threshold-miscalibration argument credible but post-hoc; it does not rescue H-004, which fails on several independent pre-registered criteria. Pre-registration was confirmed: H-004 precedes R-019/R-020 (controls R-003/R-005 are reused historical runs).
  - next: Engineer builds v3_grasp_lift (validate + at most 3 smoke episodes); the planner then picks the H-010 test size (the full design is seeds 0-7 on T0+T1 = 96 episodes; cheaper alternatives such as seeds 0-3 = 48 must be weighed against the 397 left). Rung-2 X-019 is re-run after the run_sim_batch error is fixed; rung-4 X-022 follows.
- **D-005** (PI), cites RS-005: Rung 3 (real gripper grasp) is PARKED after two failed fixes, per the two-fix rule. X-025 (the 96-episode H-010 test of v3_grasp_lift) is WITHDRAWN and will not run; H-010's premise (that the retreat knocks the block) was contradicted by per-phase telemetry before any batch. The episode budget moves to rungs 4 (v4_place / v4_mpc) and 2 (v2_walk). The weld grasp stays in use as an idealization in all results. Proposed next rung-3 experiment, for when budget allows: redesign the grasp geometry so the jaw clears the block body. Either the block handle is lengthened so the pinch centres on it, or the transport loss of the centred pinch (x offset 0) is fixed with a calibrated, non-oracle place offset. Release also needs to happen with the block seated, not 8 mm above it.
  - rationale: RS-005 (accepted): v3_grasp T1 0/48, T0 0.75 [0.61, 0.85]. Fix 1 (v3_grasp_lift, 3 smoke episodes): per-phase logs showed the block moves during gripper OPENING (top blocks 25-29 cm, bases 2.5-5.4 cm), not during lift/retreat, so the lift addresses the wrong phase; 0/3. Fix 2 (v3_grasp_release, 6 smoke episodes, seed 1): instrumented contact impulses show the opening jaw collides with the block body (T0 205 N*s body vs 7 N*s handle; about 6 body contacts vs 1 handle contact at closure), plus an 8 mm drop at release. A centred pinch (x offset 0) removes the kick (0.2 mm) but loses transport accuracy (6.5 cm). A slow opening, partial opening, seating, and opening during a 4 cm withdrawal each fixed one part, but the final candidate still failed on T1-dev-01 (upper block 6.9 cm off before release, then 11 cm during opening); 0/6. These are smoke diagnostics, not reviewed batches, so no rate claim is made from them; they justify parking, not refuting, the gripper approach.
  - next: After the lab MCP server reload (needed so new batches use the current code): (1) X-019 rung-2 v2_walk on T0, 24 episodes; (2) one v4_mpc smoke episode with the corrected bridge (direct pose copy, per-scene goal, lab-sized block proxy instead of the 0.508 m stock proxy), then X-022 on T0, 24 episodes, under H-011; (3) X-014 v4_place resumes once RS-009 is accepted.
- **D-006** (PI), cites RS-009: Default MuJoCo physics (pyramidal cone, impratio 1) stays the lab default for all current rungs. X-014 (v4_place vs v0) resumes under default physics as planned (reduced: seeds 0-3, all 34 T1-T3 dev scenes, about 136 episodes, reusing the v0 controls). H-005 is refuted for (a) within scope; (b) is not supported. H-007 stays exploratory. The ~2 cm scripted placement miss is treated as arm/controller error, which is the premise of rung 4. Creep (E-009) must be re-tested before any rung that loads tilted contacts or relies on friction holds (real grasp, pushing, walking with a carried block). Any method using elliptic physics must be compared with a control under the same physics.
  - rationale: RS-009 (accepted by the reviewer; supersedes RS-008, which was rejected for overclaiming): across 40 paired settled-stack dev scenes at seed 0, elliptic+impratio 10 changed v0's per-scene median position error by -0.003 cm (approx 95% CI [-0.006, +0.001]; every scene within [-0.059, +0.015] cm; max per-block change 0.11 cm), well inside the pre-registered +/-0.3 cm band. Success was 39/40 in both arms (Wilson [0.87, 1.00]) with no scene flipping. The same-fingerprint sensitivity on R-022's 28 scenes gives the same conclusion. v3_grasp slip/drop was 1/18 in both arms (unresolved). Scope is limited to these scenes at seed 0; this does not refute creep in general. Design: pre-registered treatment (H-005 02:48:45 before R-022..R-025), reused historical control (R-021).
  - next: X-014 resumes now: a 1-episode server-freshness/reproduction probe (v0 T1-dev-01 seed 0 must match R-021 exactly AND its log must contain the new final_pose/target_pose fields), then v4_place seeds 0-3 on the 34 T1-T3 dev scenes under H-006. X-019 (v2_walk) and X-022 (v4_mpc) follow once the lab server runs the current code.
- **D-007** (PI), cites RS-010: Adopt v4_place (closed-loop weld placement) as the placement layer for combined methods on T1-T3: 135/136 [0.96,1.00] vs v0 133/136 [0.94,0.99] at matched scenes, seeds 0-3, and it removes the pick-side lateral bias that broke rung 5 on T1. It does NOT count as rung 4 (lab-chosen placement via MPC): it reads the true block pose from the simulator (oracle_block_pose), which stays listed as an idealization. H-006 is refuted on its own mechanism criteria (residual ~1.5 cm x-shortfall, medians >= 1.5 cm). Rung 4 proper remains v4_mpc (X-022, gated on the start-pose fix).
  - rationale: Reviewer accepted RS-010: matched controls, CIs reported, pre-registration confirmed by timestamps, both idealization changes disclosed. Success meets the 0.8 target on every tier but improvement over v0 is unresolved (McNemar p=0.50, 10-12 scenes per tier); the useful property is the removed lateral bias, confirmed by v45 reaching T1 24/24 in X-029. The remaining x-shortfall is a diagnosable arm/IK issue being investigated at no episode cost.
  - next: X-032: v2_walk with the heading fix (H-012) on T0-dev-01..06 seeds 0-3 plus T1-dev-01..06 seeds 0-1 (36 eps), run serially to avoid the load-dependent walk outcomes found in RS-011, pinned to the commit with w_heading=160. In parallel: v4_place x-shortfall diagnosis, v4_mpc start-pose fix (last attempt before setting MPC aside), X-029 analysis for rung 5 off-grid.
- **D-008** (PI), cites RS-013: Rung 2 (walking approach) is NOT cleared on T0 by the pre-fix v2_walk (20/24, Wilson [0.64, 0.93], all 4 failures 'unreachable' at 77-179 deg heading error; H-009 refuted). Adopt three changes for every Sumo-based method from now on: (1) heading weight w_heading 8 -> 160 in spot_walk_approach (H-012); (2) deterministic Sumo planner: full fixed-step rollout horizon instead of the 0.125 s wall-clock cutoff, Eigen/ONNX pinned to 1 thread, episode seed passed to Sumo; (3) max_workers=1 for Sumo batches. The 28 X-032 episodes run before the restart (R-036..R-043) are exploratory only: R-036..R-041 ran the old wall-clock-truncated planner, R-042..R-043 the new planner, all on 8 workers, so they are not one behaviourally identical method. Valid clean rung-2 evidence so far: R-044/R-045 (bit-identical repeat of T0-dev-02 s1) and R-046 (T1-dev-03..06 seeds 0-1, 8/8 [0.68, 1.00], worst top block 2.42 cm, but only 2 distinct walks). Provenance gap: the run fingerprint hashes only tracked diffs and is stamped at record time, so it misses untracked files and the native Sumo build; until fixed, every run must start from a committed tree and the runner must log the native build time.
  - rationale: RS-013 (reviewer-accepted) shows the failure mechanism is heading, not position (complete separation, yaw 77-179 deg vs <= 68 deg), and that the walk was not reproducible per (scene, seed) under the old planner (shown for one episode; contention is only a candidate cause). The engineer's audit found the wall-clock rollout cutoff as a mechanism and replaced it; R-044/R-045 confirm bit-identical serial repeats. Because the walk goal is a fixed nominal pose, the walk does not depend on the scene: the effective n for walking is the number of distinct seeds, which any rung-2 design must account for.
  - next: Clean rung-2 verdict under H-012 (or a pre-filed amendment), max_workers=1, committed tree, at most 40 episodes; the experiment_planner is choosing between the literal H-012 rerun (T0 seeds 0-3, 4 distinct walks) and more distinct seeds. In parallel: v46_vlm_measure Gate 0 (offline, free) for rung 5 under H-013, with the human's guard that lateral measurement comes only from target-picture pixels.

Rung 5 is NOT cleared on T2/T3 off-grid: v45 7/16 vs v0 15/16. Most of that gap is perception (the 6.5 cm grid, staircase misread as a stack). H-008 stays refuted on clauses (b) and (c). Descriptive only, not a rung claim:

Rung 5 stays cleared on T0 and on T1 grid-aligned scenes only, per D-009.

That contradicts D-009's working assumption that v0 is a ~0.94 off-grid ceiling: the scripted-arm bias of about 2 cm topples staircases and offset columns. - **D-011** (PI), cites RS-017: Rung 2 (walking approach, v2_walk with w_heading=160) is CLEARED on the success target for T0 only: 16/16 = 1.00, Wilson 95% CI [0.81, 1.00], against a fresh matched v0 at 16/16 on the same commit 1f465a4 and fingerprint 0d729d0e (R-080 vs R-081, RS-017, reviewer ACCEPT). T1 is 4/4, too few episodes to count. H-014 and H-012 stay REFUTED on their XY mechanism clause: T0-dev-01 seed 6 ended its walk 3.50 cm off in XY, over the 3 cm limit, and the heading part held (max yaw 7.78°). Paired block error vs v0 has a median of −0.11 cm, so walking doesn't hurt placement today. That is only because the sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge and the scripted arm absorb the offset. v2_walk is therefore adopted as the rung-2 component with a known XY-drift risk. It will be combined with other rungs only after a walk XY fix is tested. Idealizations still in use for rung 2: oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision, sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge, and a fixed nominal start and goal pose.
  - rationale: The ladder counts a rung when the method meets >= 0.8 on the tier's dev scenes with a CI. The reviewer confirmed this for T0 independently of the mechanism clause. The pre-registered mechanism refutation still stands. It predicts a failure once the state bridge is removed, so the XY fix is the next rung-2 work rather than a blocker for other rungs.
  - next: The planned order continues on one frozen commit, which robotics_engineer is preparing: X-043 (rung-4 MPC T0, 48 eps, Sumo), then X-045 (rung-5 off-grid, 144 eps) and X-042 (rung-3 lift grasp, 192 eps) in parallel, then X-044. In parallel, method_designer drafts a rung-2 successor hypothesis: a final XY settling term with the heading weight kept, tested from varied start poses. Rung 2 on T1 needs >= 16 episodes before it counts.
- **D-012** (PI), cites RS-019, RS-018: RUNG 3 (real grasp) is PAUSED. The lift was delivered: 0/96 episodes below 0.12 m achieved lift. This is the second failed fix on rung 3 (H-004, then H-010), so under lab policy rung 3 moves to proposed next experiments.
RUNG 4: H-011 is OPEN as "not cleared; method-as-described untested". The reviewer REJECTED RS-018 as a refutation: the implemented Sumo pusher failed decisively (R-082 0/24 [0.00, 0.14] vs a fresh v0 R-083 24/24 [0.86, 1.00]), but it never grasped, contradicting its own weld_grasp / no_arm_block_collision idealizations. H-011's open status supersedes the "refutes H-011" field on RS-018. The v4_mpc idealizations list is wrong and must be corrected in the successor.
  - rationale: Rung 3: H-010 was genuinely tested, and it failed on outcome. The post-hoc diagnosis (RS-019, labelled post-hoc by the reviewer) moves the defect from the retreat phase (RS-005) to finger-opening: the hand sinks ~5 cm and swings 3-4 cm, knocking the second block off the stack in 34/48 T1 episodes, and T0-dev-02 drops during carry (7 episodes). The lift adds 1.2-1.9 cm of lateral tip drift, which does slight harm (angle error 7.04° vs 5.61°). Two fixes have now targeted the wrong phase, so policy says to stop spending on this rung and climb elsewhere. Caveat: matched pairs end in identical poses, so each tier effectively has 6 scenes.
Rung 4: the construct gap means no claim can be made about MPC placement with a weld grasp. The code-derived root cause (approach weight 6/m vs 220/m placement, 24 CEM samples, 1 iteration) is untested. The 0.87 cm smoke cited in H-011 ran on uncommitted code with default geometry, so it is not evidence.
  - next: Running now and next: (1) X-046 (rung 2, H-016, walk XY settling): a 32-episode A-vs-B nominal screen, then the winner, v2_walk and v0 on 28 varied starts, on the walk-xy commit being frozen. (2) Rung 5: await review of RS-020; the successor anchors the measured layout to the table/world frame (it fixes the whole-structure lateral offset loss). (3) Rung 4: a successor of H-011 that matches its stated idealizations (weld attach, verified by smoke before the run) with approach shaping and per-step cost logging, T0 vs a fresh v0, on one frozen commit. PROPOSED, not run now: rung 3 v3_grasp_hold, which holds the hand pose (impedance/position hold) during finger-opening, opens slowly, and lifts with no lateral drift, tested on T0/T1 seeds 0-7 vs a fresh v3_grasp.
- **D-013** (PI), cites RS-020: X-045 is VOID under H-015 rule A3. The reviewer REJECTED RS-020: the primary run R-084 itself has unmatched blocks with e=null (T3-dev-19 s1, T3-dev-20 s0). A3 says "any v46 episode", and my ruling to narrow it to Arm B came after the results were known, so it was unsound and is withdrawn. H-013 and H-015 stay OPEN, with neither supported nor refuted. Descriptive findings kept as exploratory, not confirmatory:
- Arm B: v46 12/16 [0.51, 0.90] vs fresh v0 15/16 [0.72, 0.99], unresolved (McNemar p=0.375).
- The white-block bridge fix held (0.16 cm and 0.83 cm).

Process: the unplanned duplicate v46 batch (R-085) can't enlarge the sample. Runner guard commit b42df27 now caps episodes per experiment.
Also: H-017 is WITHDRAWN (superseded by H-018, the H-011 amendment). Do not run it.
  - rationale: Pre-registered void rules bind as written. A3 cannot be narrowed after seeing results, even though missing-block episodes can never have a per-block e. That is a design flaw in A3 itself, to be fixed in the successor before any run, not patched now. Placement residual p was pre-registered as logged but was only derived afterwards, so it's exploratory. The anchoring defect is a clear, testable mechanism, and fixing it is the cheapest route to clearing rung 5 off-grid on T2.
  - next: Rung 5 successor (method_designer): v47, which anchors the measured layout to the table/world frame instead of re-centring on y=0. Pre-register before any run:
(a) a missing-block rule: an unmatched block counts as a perception failure with e reported as missing, and that does NOT void the experiment; void only if telemetry is absent for a MATCHED block;
(b) per-block e AND placement residual p logged by the code;
(c) cached=false on every episode;
(d) a FRESH unseen confirmatory scene set, since T2-dev-20..23 and T3-dev-21..24 were inspected in RS-020, against a fresh matched v0 on one frozen commit, with one primary batch only.

- A (v2_walk_xy): ineligible, s1 yaw -11.14°. Success 16/16 [0.81, 1.00].
- B (v2_walk_settle, linear 160 + quadratic 20000): ineligible. Post-settle, 8/16 walks have yaw >= 10° (max 36.7°); the settle phase adds +8.7° mean yaw [2.7, 14.7]; and the walk-end XY at s6 is 4.07 cm. Success 15/16 [0.72, 0.99]. Its failure (T0-dev-04 s9) comes from the settle turning the base ~40°, after which the arm misses by 19.5 cm.
Stage 2 (varied starts) is cancelled. The walk-precision mechanism has now failed twice (H-012/H-014, then H-016), so under lab policy it moves to PROPOSED next experiments. Rung 2 itself stays CLEARED on T0 and T1 (D-011, D-014) under the current idealizations (sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge, fixed nominal walk start), which hide walk drift from placement.
  - rationale: Adding position terms trades XY against heading: A's terminal-XY weight and B's settle phase each bring back yaw error. The original v2_walk (heading weight 160) has the best yaw but a 3.5 cm XY tail. No candidate meets both bounds, so further tuning without a new mechanism isn't warranted. The drift matters only once the state bridge or fixed start is removed, so it doesn't block climbing the other rungs. A combined method should use v2_walk as is, with the drift recorded as a known risk.
  - next: PROPOSED, not run now: v2_walk_xy plus a terminal heading term (or a pose-tracking final step), screened on T0 seeds 0-15 and then on varied starts. Next run: X-051's verdict (v47, rung 5), then H-018 (rung 4) once its gate passes. After that, X-049: a combined rungs 1+2+5 method (support_sort + v2_walk + v47_vlm_anchor) on T0/T1 vs fresh v0, pre-registered first.

Median perception error is 0.09 cm, with per-scene mean <= 0.11 cm on large-offset scenes. The whole-structure lateral-offset defect from v46 is fixed. H-013 and H-015 are superseded by H-019.
Caveats: n=16 per tier group and 8 scenes; v47 = v0 (superiority not required or claimed). New idealization: known_target_camera.

With rungs 1, 2 and 5 now all cleared alone, combining them is the next ladder step. Rungs 3 (paused after 2 failed fixes) and 4 (H-018 in build) stay as they are.

Rung 4 needs cost changes (release-band height term, sliding penalty, tighter target region) via a new amendment, so it's deferred under the two-failed-fixes policy.

Final ladder status:

Caveats carried: superiority over v0 is not established; the 6-worker serial/parallel parity is shown on T0/T1 but not on T2/T3; and idealizations remain (oracle_block_pose for the placement correction, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision, sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge, fixed_nominal_walk_start, clean_distinct_colour_renders, x_zero, yaw_zero, z_from_layer, known_target_camera). The scripted arm's placement tail is now the dominant failure on T2/T3.

## Results

- **RS-001** runs R-002: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-003: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-004: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-005: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-006: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48); R-007: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48); R-008: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48); R-009: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48)
  - interpretation: X-001, 24 dev scenes x 8 seeds, n=48 per tier per arm (Wilson 95% CI).
v1_support_sort (R-002/004/006/008): T0 48/48 = 1.00 [0.926, 1.000]; T1 48/48 = 1.00 [0.926, 1.000]; T2 46/48 = 0.958 [0.860, 0.988]; T3 46/48 = 0.958 [0.860, 0.988]; overall 188/192 = 0.979 [0.948, 0.992].
v0 control (R-003/005/007/009): exactly the same numbers on every tier.
v1 - v0 gap: 0.00 on T0, T1, T2 and T3. The pairing is exact: all 192 matched (scene, seed) episodes have the same outcome and the same per-block pos_err, ang_err and pre_release_err down to the last float. There are 0 discordant pairs, so the paired difference is exactly 0 and this is not a noisy estimate. H-001's quantitative criteria are met: every tier's lower CI bound (0.86) is above 0.8, overall 0.979 >= 0.9, and the gap is within 0.05 on every tier.
Support-order falsifier (second falsifier): not triggered. v1's logged plan is the identity order b0..bN in every T2/T3 episode I read (96 per arm), and it equals the spec's oracle_order. In the 6 specs I opened (T1-dev-01, T2-dev-01, T3-dev-01/03/06, plus the bridge and pyramid patterns), every block's 'on' supporters have a lower index, so the identity order is a valid support order. T0/T1 order was only checked through the identical outcomes. I did not open all 24 specs one by one.
WHY v1 == v0: the dev specs list blocks bottom-up, so a stable topological sort returns the input order, and that is the oracle order. On this dev set, v1 is behaviourally the same program as v0. This experiment shows support_sort does not break anything. It does not show that support_sort can recover an order the input listing doesn't already give it. The settle test was never asked to reject a prefix.
Control matching: R-009 (v0 T3) ran at 911b67d+dirty.3d4115ec while R-008 (v1 T3) ran at cbd9b16. All 48 per-episode outcomes and error values still match exactly, so the control is matched IN OUTCOME on T3. Provenance is not clean, because the dirty diff is unrecorded. R-003, R-005 and R-007 are at cbd9b16 and fully matched. Recommended: rerun v0 T3 at cbd9b16 (48 eps, about 2 min) to clean up the provenance.
Idealizations still in use: oracle_structure_spec, fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision. v0 also uses oracle_build_order, and v1 removes only that one.
RUN.md: runs/1791061398_v1_support_sort/RUN.md (R-006), runs/1791061982_v0/RUN.md (R-007), runs/1791062040_v1_support_sort/RUN.md (R-008), runs/1791062628_v0/RUN.md (R-009).
  - failures: 4 failures per arm, all labelled placement_error, on the same (scene, seed) pairs in both arms: T2-dev-01 s5, T2-dev-01 s7, T3-dev-03 s2, T3-dev-06 s1. No order, collapse or grasp failures. Rendered and inspected with codex (prompts/analyze_rollout/v1.md):
(1) T2-dev-01 s7 [runs/1791061398_v1_support_sort/rollout_T2-dev-01_s7.gif]: red pillar b0 was already off target before release (pre_release 3.5 cm), with final error 3.9 cm against a 3 cm tolerance. It stands upright and stable, and the bridge holds. Category: translational placement miss at place time. T2-dev-01 s5 is the same pattern (b0 3.1 cm, a marginal miss).
(2) T3-dev-03 s2 [runs/1791062040_v1_support_sort/rollout_T3-dev-03_s2.gif]: b2 (base cube) was misaligned before release (3.2 cm), final 3.4 cm. b0/b1 drift a little, to 2.6-2.7 cm. Nothing collapses. Codex: a placement offset at the place phase. Category: translational placement miss.
(3) T3-dev-06 s1 [runs/1791062040_v1_support_sort/rollout_T3-dev-06_s1.gif]: codex sees no visible failure. b3 logs ang_err 89.97 deg, but b3 is a cube and a 90 deg yaw cannot be told apart from 0. This looks like a metric artifact: ang_err does not account for cube symmetry. The episode still fails on its own, because b5 has 3.3 cm / 12 deg, which is over both tolerances (3 cm / 10 deg). Category: marginal placement miss on top of a probable false-positive angular error.
Probable common cause: the scripted place has a systematic offset of about 2 cm (median block error 2.1 cm, pre_release_err 1.5-2.5 cm on nearly every block). That leaves about 1 cm of margin, and per-seed noise pushes the tail past 3 cm. This is a controller/calibration issue in the shared place stage. It is not caused by order planning, which is consistent with v1 and v0 failing on exactly the same episodes.
  - review: accept by reviewer: ACCEPT, narrowly. A matched v0 baseline is present for every tier (R-003/005/007/009) on the same six dev scenes and seeds 0-7 as v1 (R-002/004/006/008); all referenced runs are final_eval=false and use only T0-T3 dev scenes, so no held-out scene appears in RS-001. Independent recomputation gives both arms 188/192 = 0.9792 with Wilson 95% CI [0.9477, 0.9919], tier counts 48/48, 48/48, 46/48, 46/48, matching the result; per-tier Wilson CIs are reported, with the worst lower bound 0.860 > 0.8. The paired observations are identical, with zero discordances, supporting only no observed degradation on this matched dev set. Caveat (a) is decisive for scope: because inputs are already bottom-up and support_sort returns the oracle identity order, this is a degenerate/no-op ablation; it does not demonstrate that the planner can infer/recover ordering. Caveat (b) weakens provenance for T3 because R-009 is from 911b67d+dirty rather than cbd9b16, but its 48 episode outcomes and aggregate error metrics exactly match R-008; acceptable for this narrow empirical claim, though a clean rerun is warranted. Caveat (c) does not alter success classification because T3-dev-06 s1 also fails on b5, but the symmetry-unaware angular metric must be fixed before claims where cube yaw determines outcomes. Listed idealizations are complete: oracle_structure_spec, fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision; v0 additionally uses oracle_build_order. Claim is limited to: under those idealizations, on these 24 dev scenes and seeds 0-7, replacing v0 oracle order with v1_support_sort produced no observed change and met the prespecified dev success thresholds. It is not evidence of general planner ordering ability, robustness to shuffled listings, held-out performance, or real-world performance.
  - surprise: (a) v1 and v0 are bit-identical on all 192 paired episodes, because the dev specs are listed bottom-up and support_sort returns the oracle order unchanged. The ablation is degenerate on dev and does not exercise the planner. To test H-001's mechanism, rerun on specs with shuffled block listing order or ids. (b) R-009 at a dirty commit reproduces R-008 float-for-float, so the dirty diff did not touch the T3 sim path. (c) ang_err is not symmetry-aware: a cube rotated 90 deg logs a 90 deg error (T3-dev-06 s1 b3). This can inflate failures once yaw noise grows, so fix it before rung 5. (d) There is a systematic ~2 cm placement bias across all tiers, and it, not ordering, is the binding failure mode.
- **RS-002** runs R-013: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-016: 0.67 [0.53, 0.78] (n=48); R-014: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48); R-015: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48); R-003: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-005: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-007: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48); R-012: 0.96 [0.86, 0.99] (n=48); R-010: 1.00 [0.44, 1.00] (n=3)
  - interpretation: X-004, rung 5: v5_vlm (VLM spec, oracle order, scripted_weld) vs v0 (oracle spec), with matched scenes and seeds 0-7, n=48 per tier per arm.
Pooled: v5 0.896 [0.845, 0.932] vs v0 0.979 [0.948, 0.992].
By tier: T0 1.00 vs 1.00. T2 0.958 [0.86, 0.99] vs 0.958. T3 0.958 [0.86, 0.99] vs 0.958 (same 46/48 in each arm).
T1: 0.667 [0.525, 0.783] vs 1.00 [0.926, 1.0]. Difference -0.33, Newcombe 95% CI [-0.47, -0.20].
CAVEAT ON n: the outcome is constant within a scene (8/8 or 0/8), so the effective sample size is 6 scenes per tier, not 48 episodes. At scene level T1 is 4/6 (Wilson [0.30, 0.90]) vs 6/6. As an estimate of the population T1 rate, the T1 gap is NOT resolved. The mechanism, however, is deterministic and fully accounted for (below).

(1) INTEGRITY: PASS. I compared the VLM cache (record/model_cache/<model_key>.json, keys taken from the episode logs) with scenes/dev/*.json. On T0-dev-01..06 and T3-dev-01..06 the VLM spec equals the oracle EXACTLY in block count, type, color and xyz. Every v5 episode logs perceive.cached=true with a model_key, and the plan uses VLM ids (p0..pn) rather than the oracle's b0..bn. So perception is not bypassed. The identical median errors (T0 2.3523 cm, T3 2.01697 cm) follow from identical specs fed to a deterministic sim. The sub-0.01 mm per-block differences (e.g. 1.9111 vs 1.9134 cm) are consistent with the code change from cbd9b16 to 911b67d, and R-010 gates that. T0-dev-03 and T0-dev-04 share one cache key because their targets are identical (green brick at the origin), which is benign. T2 caches were not checked individually.

(2) CAUSE ON T1: the error is the lateral offset of the top block, quantized by the VLM. The VLM outputs only y in {0, +/-0.065} (the half-grid). Height, block type, color and support are all correct.
- dev-01 (cube_on_brick): truth y=-0.0368, VLM -0.065, error -2.82 cm.
- dev-02 (stack): truth y=+0.0233, VLM 0, error -2.33 cm.
- dev-05 (cube_on_brick): truth y=-0.0513, VLM -0.065, error -1.37 cm.
All three are within 3 cm, which is why method_designer's eval passed.

Reconciliation: success depends on the vector sum of the perception error and the scripted arm's ~2 cm residual placement offset, not on either magnitude alone. Solving |b+e| for the y-component of the arm bias, using the measured v0 and v5 errors on b1:
- dev-01: v0 2.36, v5 2.25 cm, b_y = +1.5 cm.
- dev-02: v0 2.36, v5 4.47 cm, b_y = -1.9 cm.
- dev-05: v0 2.55, v5 3.58 cm, b_y = -1.6 cm.
The sign of b_y tracks the side b1 is picked from (start y = +0.32 for dev-01, -0.41 for dev-02, -0.43 for dev-05). The arm under-travels toward the pick side. On dev-01 the VLM's -y error CANCELS the bias (v5 is better than v0 there). On dev-02 and dev-05 it ADDS. A scalar "max perception error < 3 cm" check therefore cannot predict sim success. The perception budget is about 1 cm in the worst direction, not 3 cm.
b0 errors are identical across arms (VLM b0 = oracle).

(3) VIDEO (render_rollout R-016 T1-dev-02 s0, codex analyze_rollout): "orange cube released on top of blue cube with a clear lateral offset and substantial overhang; remains upright and stable; no tipping, sliding, collapse or arm collision." This is a pure placement-target error, not a dynamics failure.

H-003 (snap hybrid) would NOT fix this. Its snap keeps the VLM lateral offset for single-support blocks, so it passes the same 0 / -0.065 values through. Forcing y=0 would leave dev-02 unchanged, make dev-05 worse (5.1 cm perception error) and make dev-01 worse (3.7 cm). The fix needs continuous lateral estimation (geometric, depth or edge-based refinement), or closed-loop placement that removes the pick-side arm bias.

Idealizations still in use: oracle_build_order, fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision. Also, implicitly: yaw=0 and x=0 by scene construction, a clean rendered target image, dev T0/T2/T3 targets lying exactly on the 6.5 cm grid that the VLM already outputs, and determinism-based reuse of the cbd9b16 controls.
  - failures: v5: 20 failures, all placement_error.
- T1: 16. T1-dev-02 (8/8 seeds, b1 at 4.42-4.48 cm) and T1-dev-05 (8/8, b1 at 3.52-3.62 cm). In both, the bottom block is fine (1.6-2.0 cm). Cause: the VLM quantized the top block's lateral offset (2.33 / 1.37 cm error) in the same direction as the pick-side arm bias (~1.6-1.9 cm), so the errors summed past the 3 cm limit. The video shows a clean, stable, offset placement with no tipping or collision.
- T2: 2 and T3: 2. These match v0's 2+2 failures on the same tiers (same counts; the VLM specs are identical to the oracle on T3). That is the inherited v0 scripted-arm ~2 cm offset, not perception.
v0: 4 failures (T2 2, T3 2), all placement_error.
Categories: perception lateral quantization x arm bias = 16. Inherited scripted-placement offset = 4 (in each arm). Count, type, height and ordering errors = 0.
  - review: accept by reviewer: ACCEPT, narrowly scoped. A v0 control exists for every tier and is matched to v5_vlm on the same six dev scenes per tier and seeds 0-7: R-003/R-005/R-007 plus the clean T3 rerun R-012 versus R-013/R-016/R-014/R-015. R-010 is correctly used only as a three-episode code-drift/determinism gate and is not pooled into either arm. All cited evaluation runs are final_eval=false and name only T0-T3 dev scenes, so no held-out scene was used. Independent recomputation matches RS-002 exactly: v5 is 172/192 = 0.8958, episode-level Wilson 95% CI [0.8446, 0.9316], with tier counts 48/48, 32/48, 46/48, 46/48 and 20 placement errors; v0 is 188/192 = 0.9792, CI [0.9477, 0.9919], with 48/48, 48/48, 46/48, 46/48 and 4 placement errors. Those episode-level CIs are not valid evidence of a population-level T1 gap because outcomes are constant across the eight seeds within each scene. RS-002 explicitly recognizes the effective n=6 scenes per tier, reports v5 T1 as 4/6 with Wilson CI about [0.30, 0.90] versus 6/6 control, and correctly phrases the population-rate difference as unresolved rather than significant. Its deterministic, on-this-dev-set failure-pattern account is supported by the observed scene clustering, but must not be generalized beyond these scenes. The remaining idealizations are disclosed completely for the comparison: oracle_build_order, fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision; v0 additionally has oracle_structure_spec, while v5 uses cached VLM perception. The result also lists the important implicit restrictions: yaw=0 and x=0 scene construction, clean rendered targets, grid-aligned T0/T2/T3 targets, and determinism-based reuse of older controls. Therefore the justified claim is limited to the measured dev-set behavior and an unresolved scene-population T1 gap; it is not held-out or real-world evidence.
  - surprise: 1) The VLM output is effectively quantized to the 6.5 cm half-grid ({0, +/-0.065, +/-0.13}). It is exact on grid-aligned tiers (T0/T2/T3 dev) and makes rounding errors on T1's continuous offsets. What was reported as a "0.5-2.8 cm lateral offset error" is rounding. The T0/T3 dev scenes therefore cannot test fine pose at all.
2) Perception error magnitude is anti-predictive at this resolution. The largest error (dev-01, 2.82 cm) passes and gives LOWER b1 error than the oracle, because it cancels the scripted arm's pick-side bias. Smaller errors fail when they align with the bias.
3) The scripted arm has a ~1.5-1.9 cm lateral bias whose sign follows the pick side. That is a systematic v0 property hidden by the oracle spec, and it consumes most of the 3 cm budget.
4) The effective n is the number of scenes (6 per tier), not episodes: outcomes are 8/8 or 0/8 per scene.
- **RS-003** runs R-018: 0.98 [0.93, 0.99] (n=128); R-017: 0.98 [0.93, 0.99] (n=128)
  - interpretation: X-011 (H-001, rung 1). v1_support_sort (R-018) vs v0 control (R-017) on 16 shuffled dev scenes (T1-dev-07..12, T2-dev-07..10, T3-dev-07..12), seeds 0-7, matched pairs, both at commit 911b67d+dirty.f946d937.

ORDER FALSIFIER (decisive, deterministic): NOT TRIGGERED, 16/16 scenes. I read all 128 v1 episode logs. The planned order is constant across the 8 seeds of every scene. I opened all 16 scene specs and checked each order against the 'on' relations: every block comes after all of its supporters on every scene. The listed spec order violates support on all 16, because the first-listed block is a supported block (e.g. T1: b0 'on' b1 is listed first; T3-dev-09: b3 'on' b4,b2 is listed first). So support_sort did real reordering work this time, unlike in RS-001. Orders: T1-07..12 [b1,b0]; T2-07 [b0,b2,b1], T2-08 [b1,b0,b2], T2-09 [b2,b1,b0], T2-10 [b1,b0,b2]; T3-07 [b3,b0,b1,b2], T3-08 [b2,b1,b0], T3-09 [b4,b2,b1,b3,b5,b0], T3-10 [b3,b1,b2,b0], T3-11 [b2,b1,b0], T3-12 [b4,b3,b1,b0,b2,b5]. Each one equals the spec's oracle_order exactly.

SUCCESS (Wilson 95%, episode level; v1 = v0 on every tier):
- T1: 48/48 = 1.00 [0.926, 1.000]
- T2: 30/32 = 0.9375 [0.798, 0.983]
- T3: 47/48 = 0.979 [0.891, 0.996]
- Overall: 125/128 = 0.977 [0.933, 0.992]
Paired v1 - v0 = 0.000. The 3 failed pairs are float-identical in both arms, and the aggregate medians match to the last digit. That follows from v1's order equalling oracle_order on every scene: the two arms execute the same program.

EFFECTIVE n: outcomes are NOT constant within a scene. T2-dev-07 is 6/8 and T3-dev-09 is 7/8, so episode-level CIs are not wholly degenerate. They are still clustered, and the honest count of independent units is scenes: T1 6, T2 4, T3 6. At scene level ('all 8 seeds pass'): T1 6/6 [0.61, 1.00], T2 3/4 [0.30, 0.95], T3 5/6 [0.44, 0.97]. On top of that, these 16 scenes are id-relabelled copies of dev-01..06. The geometry is unchanged (T1-dev-07's top-cube y = -0.0368 is T1-dev-01's), so the distinct structures are 6/4/6. With n < 20 scenes per tier, the success-rate claim is NOT resolved at population level. Only the deterministic order claim is settled for these 16 specs.

X-011's stricter plan, 'Wilson LB >= 0.8 with 1 T2 failure', did not hold: T2 had 2 failures, LB 0.798, so it misses by 0.002. The identical v0 control fails the same way, so this is the shared scripted placement stage and not the order planner. It does not count against H-001's mechanism.

Common cause: the systematic ~2 cm scripted-place offset (median pos_err 2.09 cm) leaves about 1 cm of margin, and per-seed noise pushes the tail past 3 cm. That cause is in the shared place stage, not order planning. Category counts: scripted-placement offset 3/3, ordering 0/3.

R-017 (v0) and R-018 (v1_support_sort) are a matched control pair at the same commit, with the identical 16 scenes and seeds 0-7 (128 episodes per arm). Both runs are final_eval=false and use only T1/T2/T3 dev scene IDs; no held-out scene appears. Independent recomputation of R-017+R-018 matches the records: 250/256 successes overall (0.9765625, Wilson 95% CI [0.9498, 0.9892]), six placement errors, and pooled tier counts T1 96/96, T2 60/64, T3 94/96. Per-arm values in RS-003 also match each run: 125/128, CI [0.9334, 0.9920], with 3 placement errors and identical tier counts and medians. The paired difference is exactly 0; therefore the data do not establish an improvement, and RS-003 does not claim one. It explicitly reports the episode-level CIs but correctly treats scenes as the independent units (T1 n=6, T2 n=4, T3 n=6), gives scene-level intervals, notes the scenes are ID-relabelled copies rather than new geometry, and states that population success is unresolved with n<20 per tier. The deterministic claim is limited to these 16 specs: all planned orders are support-valid. The flagged possible leak is resolved against the implementation: order_support_sort does not read scene, start, or oracle_order; it uses only spec support relations and target (z,y) as its ready-set tie-break, and a regression test verifies listing-order invariance. Its exact match to oracle_order occurs because oracle_order independently uses the same geometric (z,y) key, not because start/oracle order is read. This still means both arms execute the same order, so the experiment cannot answer whether a different valid order changes outcomes; RS-003 says so. Remaining idealizations and restrictions are disclosed: oracle_structure_spec, fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision, plus yaw/x/grid restrictions, relabelled-copy scenes, and deterministic simulation; v0 alone uses oracle_build_order. The result is justified only as evidence that support_sort recovers a valid order and matches v0 on these dev scenes, not as held-out, population-level, or real-world evidence.

The pattern fits ordering the grounded blocks by ascending target y, which matches the generator's convention. It is also consistent with the planner reading the spec's 'start' list, which is serialized in oracle_order. That would be a leak. I could not read the planner source from this workspace, so this is UNVERIFIED. Someone with code access should confirm the tie-break key. (b) Because v1 reproduced the oracle order, v1 is again bit-identical to v0. X-011's secondary goal, a paired discordance count showing whether a DIFFERENT valid order changes placement outcomes, cannot be answered here (0 discordant by construction). MECHANISM. Question: is T1 placement_error a release/retreat defect or a pose offset?
Answer: a RELEASE/RETREAT defect, not a pose offset.
Evidence:
(1) Before release the top block is placed about as well as the base. Top pre_release_err is 1.4-3.7 cm (median ~3.2) vs base 2.2-3.2 cm in the same episodes. The final top error is 20-41 cm, often flipped 90 or 180 deg, which is about 10x the pre-release error. No pose offset of 0.5 cm produces that.
(2) pre_release_err is measured on the HANGING block at place + 0.8 cm clearance, before the block seats (real_grasp.py). So ~3 cm is the hang/clearance baseline, not a lateral miss. Bases logged at 2.8-3.3 cm pre-release settle at 1.3-2.6 cm: T1-dev-01 b0 s0 3.1 -> 1.9; T0-dev-01 s0 3.3 -> 2.6.
  - Caveat: by the LITERAL pre-registered mechanism falsifier ('most failing tops pre_release_err > 3 cm'), 27/41 tops exceed 3.0 cm. I judge that threshold miscalibrated: it is 0.0-0.7 cm above the hang baseline. I do not count it as evidence for a placement miss. Flag for the reviewer.
(3) Video, codex analyze_rollout on R-020 T1-dev-01 s1: 'cube stays in place through opening, then moves during the gripper retreat; a jaw/finger visibly contacts it and knocks it off the base; base remains standing.'
(4) The same defect appears on the GROUND at T0. Codex on R-019 T0-dev-05 s3 (pre 2.4 -> final 5.9 cm): 'during the post-release retreat, an open jaw remains against the block and drags it ~3.5 cm.' The v3_grasp retreat moves back along the tool -x axis at 45 deg pitch from the place height. It does not lift clear first, so the open jaw drags through whatever it just released.

v3_grasp_stack ANALYSIS. Does it target the actual cause? PARTIALLY, and mostly not.
(a) oracle_place_correction targets the pre-release pose. That is not the cause; pre-release is already at the base-block baseline. It also adds a stronger oracle: true block pose used for closed-loop place correction.
(b) 'vertical_clearance_before_retreat' is the right subsystem, but the configured release_clear_vector_m [-0.08, 0, 0.03] is applied in the WORLD frame (real_grasp_stack.py: tip + clear_vector). It is mostly a horizontal pull: 8 cm in -x, 3 cm up, about 20 deg above horizontal, and 3 cm is less than the ~10 cm block height. The open jaw will very likely still sweep the block. The code default (no vector given) is a pure 0.10 m vertical lift, and the yaml overrides it.
(c) Nothing in it addresses the brick transit drops: 7/8 on T0-dev-02 and 7/8 for T1-dev-03 b0.
(d) Release fix scope: it is gated as a fix for 'supported placements', but T0 ground placements suffer the same drag, so the fix should apply to every release.
Recommendation:
- Run v3_grasp_stack with release_clear_vector_m = [0, 0, >= 0.12] and oracle_place_correction=false as the primary arm.
- Add pose-correction as a separate ablation, so the release fix is not confounded with a new oracle.
- Its new per-phase displacement logs (release_open / release_clear / retreat displacement) should settle the mechanism quantitatively. Pre-register: retreat_displacement > 3 cm on most failing tops.
- Separately fix the brick grasp (grip force / handle friction / pitch on bricks).
Per H-004's own decision rule (T1 general), STOP T2/T3 spend until release is fixed.

Idealizations still in use (v3): oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, oracle_block_pose (true pose for handle localization), fixed_base, scripted_arm. Implicit: yaw=0/x=0 targets, deterministic sim, per-handle friction override, and grasp_tip_offset calibrated on R-011 seed 0 (hence seed 0 is in-sample). v3_grasp_stack would add oracle place correction (true block pose fed back at place time). v0 controls additionally use weld_grasp and no_arm_block_collision; these are the two that v3 removes.
  - failures: Primary cause per failed episode, from the per-stage logs (pre_release_err, max_relative_drift, transported) plus 2 rendered videos.

T0 (12 failures, all out of sample, seeds 1-7):
- GRASP DROP/SLIP IN TRANSIT: 7. All on T0-dev-02 (single BRICK starting at y=+0.22), seeds 1-7: drift 12-45 cm, final 12-57 cm. Logged as drop x5 / slip x2.
  - Seed 0 passed but ALSO logged a slip (drift 5.9 cm), so the in-sample calibration seed passed marginally.
- RELEASE/RETREAT DRAG ON GROUND: 5. T0-dev-05 s1/s3/s6/s7 (pre 2.2-2.4 -> final 3.6-5.9 cm) and T0-dev-03 s1 (2.8 -> 3.6 cm). Video of T0-dev-05 s3: the open jaw drags the block ~3.5 cm during the retreat. Logged as placement_error.
- Scenes not affected: dev-01, dev-04, dev-06 are 8/8.

T1 (48 failures). Structure split: cube_on_brick = dev-01/03/05; stack = dev-02/04/06.
cube_on_brick (24):
- dev-01: 8/8 top knocked off after release (pre 3.0-3.6 -> final 21-40 cm). Video s1: the jaw knocks it off during retreat; the base stays standing.
- dev-03: 7/8 BRICK BASE b0 drop/slip in transit (drift 11-43 cm), plus top-cube slips (drift 5-10 cm) in 5 of them. s0 is a top knock-off (3.7 -> 35 cm).
- dev-05: 6/8 top knocked off (s2-s7, 3.2-3.4 -> 24-32 cm). In s0/s1 the BASE b0 is shoved 20-25 cm during b1's release/retreat (b1 pre-release only 1.5-2.1 cm).
=> release/retreat 17, grasp drop/slip 7.
stack (24):
- dev-02: 7/8 top knocked off and flipped 180 deg (21-34 cm). In s3 both blocks are shoved (b0 7.8, b1 5.6 cm).
  - On b1, handle_contacts_after_close = 0 in s1-s7: the handle was MISSED and the cube was carried by a body pinch. Slip flagged in s3/s5 but not logged as a failure, because pre-release was < 5 cm.
- dev-04: top knocked off in s0/2/3/5/7 (22-41 cm); base b0 shoved 6-9 cm in s1/s4/s6.
- dev-06: top knocked off in s1/3/5/6/7 (20-39 cm); base shoved 7-10 cm in s0/s2/s4.
=> release/retreat 24, grasp 0.

T1 totals: release/retreat disturbance 41/48 (cube_on_brick 17, stack 24); grasp transit drop/slip 7/48 (all T1-dev-03, the brick base).
Share of failures on cube_on_brick = 24/48 = 50%. Predicted >= 75%: FALSIFIED. Every stack scene fails 8/8, so the defect is general, not structure-specific. Pose-offset (pre-release miss) failures: 0 attributable.
Category labels as logged by the runner (multi-label per episode): T0 drop 5 / slip 2 / placement_error 5; T1 placement_error 41 / drop 6 / slip 6. Note: the runner's 'placement_error' here means post-release disturbance, not a place-time miss.
  - review: accept by reviewer: ACCEPT, narrowly and with the mechanism caveat treated as exploratory. Controls are matched on evaluation design: R-019 vs R-003 use the same six T0 dev scenes and seeds 0-7; R-020 vs R-005 use the same six T1 dev scenes and seeds 0-7. All four runs are final_eval=false and contain only dev scenes, so no held-out scene was used. Independent recomputation over R-019/R-020/R-003/R-005 gives 192 episodes, pooled success 132/192 = 0.6875 with Wilson 95% CI [0.6188,0.7488], failure labels drop=11, slip=8, placement_error=46, and tier totals T0 84/96 and T1 48/96, matching the run summaries embedded in RS-005. Per-arm metrics also match: R-019 36/48 [0.6122,0.8508], R-020 0/48 [0,0.0741], and both controls 48/48 [0.9259,1]. The result honestly identifies the effective independent n as six scenes per tier: at scene level T0 is unresolved (3/6 all-seed-pass scenes vs 6/6 control, Fisher two-sided about 0.18), while T1 is resolved on these dev geometries (0/6 vs 6/6, about 0.002), with no claim of broader geometry generalization.

The run logs substantiate the reported category counts and scene pattern: T0 has 7 drop/slip episodes on dev-02 and 5 placement_error episodes on dev-03/dev-05; T1 has 41 placement_error episodes and 7 remaining episodes on dev-03 with transit drop/slip. The two cited rollout artifacts are consistent with the causal relabel: T1-dev-01 s1 remains placed through opening and is knocked off during retreat, and T0-dev-05 s3 is dragged after release. For the remaining T1 placement_error cases, the logs show no promoted pre-release grasp failure, modest top pre-release errors (1.4-3.7 cm), then much larger final errors; thus 'post-release release/retreat disturbance' is a supported aggregate interpretation, though the videos directly demonstrate only exemplars rather than all 41 episodes.

The preregistered mechanism test is literally falsified: among the 41 T1 placement_error episodes, 27 top-block pre_release_err values exceed 3.0 cm, so a majority trip H-004's stated mechanism falsifier. The analyst's explanation is technically legitimate as a post-hoc diagnosis of a bad metric threshold: real_grasp.py records Euclidean error while the block is still hanging at a configured 0.8 cm clearance, and successful/base placements commonly sit near the threshold; the videos and large pre-to-final displacement provide evidence that the dominant physical event occurs after release. But it remains post-hoc relative to H-004 and cannot be used to declare its preregistered mechanism test passed. RS-005 is acceptable because it explicitly discloses the literal trigger and refutes H-004 rather than rescuing it.

H-004's refutation stands independently and decisively: T0 out-of-sample is 30/42, below the preregistered <38/42 falsifier, with 7 drop/slip failures >2; T1 is 0/48, below 0.5; and every same-size stack scene fails 8/8, directly triggering the preregistered 'general defect' branch and contradicting the >=75% cube_on_brick concentration (observed 24/48 = 50%). Timestamp audit confirms preregistration under the standing rule: H-004 created at 1791070753.9601228, before R-019 at 1791071342.292632 and R-020 at 1791071499.755587. The listed remaining idealizations are adequate and scoped: oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, oracle_block_pose, fixed_base, scripted_arm, plus deterministic/target and calibrated-friction assumptions; the v0 controls' weld_grasp and no_arm_block_collision are also disclosed as the intended rung contrast. The accepted claim is limited to these six dev scenes per tier under those idealizations.
  - surprise: 1) The defect is general, not structure-specific. Same-size stacks fail 24/24, as badly as cube_on_brick. Cause: the v3 retreat drags the open jaw horizontally through the released block. This happens even on the ground at T0 (T0-dev-05: 4/8, 1.5-3.5 cm drags).
2) Top-knock and base-shove trade off by where the top ends up. When the top block sits further in (pre-release 1.4-2.1 cm), the retreat shoves the BASE 6-25 cm instead (T1-dev-04/05/06). That is consistent with the jaw sweeping through the whole structure.
3) The pre-registered pre_release_err <= 3 cm mechanism test is miscalibrated. The metric is taken on a hanging block 0.8 cm above its seat, and correctly placed bases routinely log 2.8-3.3 cm. Read literally, 27/41 failing tops 'exceed' 3 cm, yet they end 20-40 cm away.
4) v3_grasp_stack's 'vertical_clearance_before_retreat' is configured with a world-frame vector [-0.08, 0, 0.03], which is mostly horizontal. The code default would have been a pure 0.10 m vertical lift. Its oracle_place_correction targets a pose error that the data says is not the cause.
5) Brick grasps are fragile and depend on start pose: T0-dev-02 fails 7/8 and T1-dev-03's brick base fails 7/8, while other bricks (T1-dev-01/05 base) carry fine. The calibration seed 0 on T0-dev-02 passed with a logged 5.9 cm slip.
6) T1-dev-02 top cube: the handle was missed on 7/8 (handle_contacts = 0) and carried by a body pinch. Slip flags were not promoted to failures, because pre-release was < 5 cm.
- **RS-006** runs R-021: 1.00 [0.76, 1.00] (n=12); R-022: 0.96 [0.82, 0.99] (n=28); R-023: 0.97 [0.87, 1.00] (n=40); R-025: 0.33 [0.16, 0.56] (n=18); R-024: 0.28 [0.12, 0.51] (n=18)
  - interpretation: X-016 physics A/B (seed 0; effective n is in scenes). Tests H-005.

PHYSICS NOTE: v0_elliptic = elliptic cone + impratio 10, with NO noslip_iterations. v3_grasp_elliptic = elliptic cone + impratio 10 + noslip_iterations 10, and re-tuned. Comparisons within a method (v0 vs v0_elliptic) change physics only. Comparisons across methods are not matched in physics. v3 vs v3_elliptic is also confounded by the re-tune. The elliptic config did take effect: v0 wall time per T0 episode doubled, about 10 s to 20 s.

(a) v0 default (R-021 + R-022, 40 scenes, 112 blocks) vs v0_elliptic (R-023, same 40 scenes, seed 0, same commit):
- Success: 39/40 in both arms, Wilson 95% CI [0.87, 1.00]. Paired scene for scene, 0 discordant scenes; the single failure is T2-dev-07 in both arms.
- Median block position error: 2.082 cm (default) vs 2.082 cm (elliptic), so Δmedian ≈ 0.00 cm.
- Per block, paired: |Δ pos_err| < 0.01 cm for the large majority of the 112 blocks, and at most 0.11 cm on any block (T3-dev-04 b1 and T3-dev-10 b1: 2.03 to 1.92 cm). Any CI on the paired median Δ therefore lies inside ±0.12 cm, well below the 0.3 cm threshold.
- Median angle error: 2.48 deg (default) vs 2.49 deg (elliptic).
- Pre-release vs post-settle: pre_release_err matches between arms to about 1e-6 m. Settle creep (post minus pre) is typically +0.1 to +0.2 cm, range about 0.05 to 0.6 cm (T2-dev-03 b0: 1.30 to 1.91 cm), and is identical under both physics models to under 0.01 cm. So about 90% of the ~2.1 cm placement error is already present while the block is still welded to the hand, i.e. arm/IK/weld offset. Creep is small and does not depend on the friction cone.
- Signed lateral error: NOT computable. episodes.jsonl logs only scalar pos_err and ang_err per block, with no final block pose, target pose, or pick-side frame. X-016 specified this field but it was never logged, so the pick-side-bias half of H-005(a) cannot be evaluated.
- H-005(a) verdict: REFUTED by its own rule (median change < 0.3 cm and success identical scene for scene). The ~2 cm miss is controller/arm error, not contact creep. This confirms the rung-4 premise (that the ~2 cm miss is arm error) rather than contradicting it.

(b) v3_grasp default (R-025) vs v3_grasp_elliptic re-tuned (R-024), 18 scenes, same commit:
- T0: 6/6 [0.61, 1.00] vs 5/6 [0.44, 0.97].
- T1: 0/12 [0, 0.24] vs 0/12 [0, 0.24].

- H-005(b) criterion (weak support needs a drop in the slip/drop count): NOT MET. With n=18 and 1 vs 1 the difference is not resolved; part (b) is at best inconclusive.
- Secondary, confounded by the re-tune: per-stage carry max_relative_drift is about 0.5 to 0.8 cm lower under elliptic (about 2.1 to 2.6 cm vs about 2.9 to 3.3 cm). Top-block pre_release_err is also lower (all 12 T1 failing blocks ≤ 2.8 cm under elliptic).
- Pick-side lateral bias for v3: not logged, same gap as in (a).
- T1 under elliptic: all 12 failures are a correctly placed second block (pre_release ≤ 3 cm in 12/12) that ends tipped at 90 or 180 deg. This is a release/retreat disturbance on every T1 structure, including the same-size stacks.

(c) Reproduction: R-025 seed 0 reproduces R-019/R-020 seed 0. T0-dev-01 pos_err is bit-identical (0.0259377648852922 m). All 12 overlapping scenes (T0-01..06, T1-01..06) match per block at RUN.md resolution (0.1 cm), with identical success and failure categories. The old v3 controls are valid on the current code.

Recommendation for X-014: RESUME UNDER DEFAULT PHYSICS as-is; do not re-plan under elliptic. The physics change moves v0 by about 0.00 cm, so v4_place would not be tuning against creep. Overall: the dominant rung-3 failure in both physics models is the release/retreat. The opened finger stays in contact with the top block and tips or drags it. It is not a carry slip, and it is general across T1 (cube_on_brick and same-size stacks alike).

The elliptic cone + impratio 10 left v0 essentially unchanged: 112 paired blocks agree within 0.11 cm, pre_release_err agrees to about 1e-6 m, and settle creep is the same, even though wall time doubled. 2. Re-tuned v3 under elliptic visibly tightens the carry (drift -0.5 to -0.8 cm, smaller top-block pre-release error), yet T1 stays 0/12. Better placement exposes the release defect more cleanly: 12/12 failures are a correctly placed top block knocked over at finger-open or retreat.

H-005 VERDICT, as filed:
(a) REFUTED. Δmedian pos_err ≈ 0.00 cm (2.082 vs 2.082), max per-block |Δ| 0.11 cm over 112 blocks and 40 paired scenes, success 39/40 vs 39/40 (Wilson [0.87, 1.00]) with 0 discordant scenes. The < 0.3 cm, identical-success refutation rule is met. The pick-side-bias clause cannot be evaluated because signed lateral error is not logged.
(b) NOT MET (no weak support). Slip/drop episodes 1/18 vs 1/18 (Wilson [0.01, 0.26]), so the predicted drop did not occur.
PREMISE ERROR IN H-005(b): H-005(b) assumed that v3_grasp_elliptic was re-tuned, so a re-tune would confound physics. That is factually wrong. methods/v3_grasp_elliptic.yaml params are identical to methods/v3_grasp.yaml; only the physics changed (elliptic, impratio 10, noslip_iterations 10). The confound H-005 used to cap (b) at "weakly supported" does not exist. This does not rescue (b), because its outcome (fewer slip/drops) did not happen either way.

EXPLORATORY / POST-HOC (H-007(b) thresholds, written after the data existed; NOT a confirmatory test):
- Arm 3 (R-025) T0 = 6/6; arm 4 (R-024) T0 = 5/6 = 0.83.
- Slip+drop count on T0+T1 depends on how it is counted:
  - by episode: 1 vs 1 (0% reduction);
  - by failure-category entries: 2 (T1-09 drop + slip) vs 1 (T0-02 drop), a 50% reduction;
  - by stage-level grasp events: 4 vs 2, a 50% reduction.
- The refutation clause "arm 4 T0 < arm 3 T0" (0.83 < 1.00) fires under every counting. The support clause fires only under the category and event countings, so those two countings would trigger support and refutation at once. The episode counting is refuted by both clauses.
- Exploratory reading: the result leans toward refuted / no benefit, and it hinges on one scene (T0-dev-02's off-center pinch). Not resolved at n = 6 T0 / 18 total scenes.
- H-007 stays open / exploratory. No status is set from this.

Physics note: v0_elliptic has no noslip_iterations, while v3_grasp_elliptic has noslip_iterations 10. Comparisons within a method change physics only; comparisons across methods are not matched.

The stored aggregate metrics recompute exactly: pooled v0 control R-021+R-022 is 39/40, Wilson 95% CI [0.8712,0.9956], median block error 2.08157 cm; R-023 is 39/40 with the same CI and 2.08203 cm. R-025 is 6/18 [0.1628,0.5625] with failures {placement_error:11, drop:1, slip:1}; R-024 is 5/18 [0.1250,0.5087] with {placement_error:12, drop:1}. All scenes are dev scenes and all runs have final_eval=false, so no held-out use is shown. R-022/R-023/R-024/R-025 use the same dirty fingerprint 911b67d+dirty.982733a0 and matched seed 0/scenes within each contrast, but reused control R-021 has a different dirty fingerprint, 911b67d+dirty.33385316; therefore 12/40 v0 control scenes are not demonstrated to be from the same worktree state as treatment. The timestamp label must be exactly 'pre-registered treatment, reused historical control': H-005 predates R-022..R-025 but postdates R-021; H-007 is post-hoc/exploratory. The reported paired sample result (median change about 0.00046 cm; observed max block |change| 0.11 cm, identical 39/40 scene outcomes) is strong evidence of no material effect in these 40 seed-0 settled-stack scenes, but it does not justify the broad H-005(a) status 'REFUTED' or 'creep does not explain the ~2 cm drift' without a scoped qualifier. No formal paired equivalence CI is recorded, seed variation is untested, and E-009's creep appears under 30-degree tilted gravity—a regime that may not occur in settled flat stacks. The defensible conclusion is that H-005(a)'s predicted >=0.5 cm benefit was not observed, and an effect >=0.3 cm is ruled out for these tested seed-0 scenes; creep in general, or under tilted contacts, is not refuted. Part (b) is correctly unresolved/not met because 1/18 versus 1/18 slip/drop episodes has no separation; v0_elliptic has no noslip while v3_grasp_elliptic has noslip_iterations=10, so cross-method attribution is invalid. Idealizations and seed-0-only scope are listed.

Design: pre-registered treatment, reused historical control.
- Treatment R-023 and the new controls R-022, R-024, R-025 were all generated after H-005 was created (02:48:45).

CLAIM (narrowed): in these 40 settled-stack dev scenes at seed 0, elliptic cone + impratio 10 had no material effect on v0's placement error or success.
- Under H-005's own pre-registered (a) criterion (|Δ median| < 0.3 cm and identical success scene for scene), H-005(a) is refuted ONLY within this scope. Part (a) concerned v0's placement drift.
- This does NOT refute the contact creep reported in E-009 in general. v0 uses a weld grasp, and its settled flat stacks never exercise tilted or loaded contacts (e.g. the 30° case), where pyramidal-cone creep is expected to matter.
- Seed variation is not covered: every arm is seed 0. RS-004 found ang_err is driven mainly by seed rather than scene, so these intervals are over scenes at a fixed seed.

PAIRED EQUIVALENCE, all 40 scenes:
d_s = per-scene median block error (elliptic R-023) minus the same scene's median (default R-021 + R-022). Computed by hand from the full-precision episodes.jsonl. Shell is denied in this read-only workspace, so no resampling was run.
- Position error, per-scene d_s:
  - mean -0.0026 cm, median -0.0002 cm.
  - range [-0.059, +0.015] cm. The extremes are T3-dev-04 (-0.059) and T3-dev-10 (-0.039), both from b1 shifting 2.03 to 1.92 cm; then T2-dev-03 (+0.015). The other 37 scenes are within ±0.01 cm.
  - Approximate 95% CI for the mean: about [-0.006, +0.001] cm (normal approximation, SD 0.012, SE 0.0018). A bootstrap over scenes should give a similar interval, possibly skewed by the two T3 outliers.
  - Guaranteed bound: any percentile-bootstrap CI of the mean or median of d_s lies inside the observed range [-0.059, +0.015] cm. That range is entirely within ±0.3 cm, so equivalence at the pre-registered ±0.3 cm margin holds whatever the resampling details.
  - Per block (112 blocks): max |Δ| = 0.11 cm.
- Angle error, per-scene d_s:
  - mean +0.014°, median about +0.003°.
  - range [-0.114°, +0.237°]. Extremes: T2-dev-06 +0.24°, T2-dev-07 +0.12°, T2-dev-03 -0.11°.
  - Approximate 95% CI for the mean: about [-0.001, +0.030]°.
  - Any bootstrap CI lies within [-0.11, +0.24]°, under 3% of the 10° tolerance.
- Success: 39/40 vs 39/40 (Wilson [0.87, 1.00]), 0 discordant scenes; T2-dev-07 fails in both arms.

SENSITIVITY, R-022's 28 scenes only:
These are T0-01..06, T1-05..12, T2-05..10 and T3-05..12. R-022 and R-023 share the fingerprint 911b67d+dirty.982733a0; R-021's 12 scenes (dirty.33385316) are excluded.
- Position d_s: mean -0.0015 cm, range [-0.039, +0.002] cm, approximate 95% CI [-0.004, +0.001] cm. Within ±0.3 cm.
- Angle d_s: mean +0.019°, range [-0.055, +0.237]°, approximate 95% CI [-0.001, +0.039]°.
- Success: 27/28 vs 27/28 (Wilson [0.82, 0.99]), 0 discordant.
- The conclusion does not change.
- Keeping R-021 is supported independently by RS-004: R-021 is bit-identical, all 41 blocks, to the seed-0 rows of the older controls R-005, R-007 and R-012, despite its different dirty tree.

Settle creep (post minus pre-release): typically +0.1 to +0.2 cm, and identical across physics to under 0.01 cm. About 90% of the ~2.1 cm error is present before release, so it is arm/IK/weld error in this scope.

PART (b): unresolved.
- Slip/drop episodes: 1/18 for R-025 (default) vs 1/18 for R-024 (elliptic), Wilson [0.01, 0.26] each.
- T0 success 6/6 vs 5/6; T1 0/12 vs 0/12.
- H-005(b) predicted fewer slip/drops, and that was not observed, so (b) is not supported. n=18 cannot resolve a difference.
- H-005(b)'s premise ("re-tune confounds physics") was factually wrong: the yaml params are identical, and only the physics changed (elliptic + impratio 10 + noslip_iterations 10).
- H-007(b)'s physics-only thresholds are exploratory/post-hoc only (see RS-008: the refutation clause fires on T0 5/6 < 6/6, the support clause depends on how slip/drop is counted, and everything hinges on T0-dev-02).

Physics note: v0_elliptic has no noslip_iterations, while v3_grasp_elliptic has noslip_iterations 10. Comparisons within a method change physics only; comparisons across methods are not matched.

Not measurable: signed lateral / pick-side bias. Only scalar pos_err is logged, with no final or target poses.

The revision limits the claim to the 40 enumerated settled-stack dev scenes at seed 0 and explicitly says it does not refute E-009 creep generally or cover seed variation. Within that finite scope, equivalence at the pre-registered ±0.3 cm margin is adequately established even without a bootstrap: all observed per-scene position differences lie in [-0.059,+0.015] cm, every observed per-block difference is at most 0.11 cm, the aggregate median difference is about 0.00046 cm, and success is paired-identical at 39/40 with zero discordant scenes. The normal mean-difference interval [-0.006,+0.001] cm is a reasonable supplementary approximation, not the sole basis of the conclusion. The statement that percentile-bootstrap statistics from the empirical sample remain within the observed range is correct for resampling these scenes, although it should not be read as a distribution-free population guarantee; RS-009 avoids that overgeneralization by defining the scope as these scenes and this seed. The same-fingerprint sensitivity excluding historical R-021 retains equivalence on R-022's 28 scenes (range [-0.039,+0.002] cm, paired success 27/28 versus 27/28), so the different dirty fingerprint cannot drive the conclusion. - T2: 10/10 [0.722, 1.0] vs 9/10 [0.596, 0.982]

Pre-registered criterion (4) (LB >= 0.8) passes at episode level but FAILS at scene level on every tier. n = 10-12 per tier is below 20, so success is unresolved.

(3) POSITION ERROR. Median v4_place is 1.60 cm pooled. Per tier it is about 1.6-1.7 cm, read from the 0.1 cm-rounded RUN.md table; the T1 median falls between 1.6 and 1.7, and T2/T3 are about 1.6. The v0 control at the same scenes is about 2.0-2.2 cm (R-005 2.13, R-007 2.13, R-012 2.02, R-017 2.09, seeds 0-7). The effect is about -0.5 cm (-25%), but every tier stays >= 1.5 cm, which is the pre-registered REFUTE threshold, and far above the < 1.0 cm SUPPORT target. No bootstrap CI on the median: the workspace is read-only, so I could not aggregate per-block errors programmatically.
DECOMPOSITION from the pose fields. I checked 6 episodes plus the failure by hand; this is NOT a full 136-episode aggregate.
- LATERAL (y) PICK-SIDE BIAS IS ESSENTIALLY GONE on the top block of the RS-002 scenes. Final minus target dy for b1:
  - T1-01: v0 +1.56 cm (R-027) vs v4 +0.33 cm (s0) / +0.32 cm (s1)
  - T1-02: RS-002 b_y -1.9 vs v4 -0.32 cm
  - T1-05: RS-002 b_y -1.6 vs v4 +0.00 cm
  All are below 0.5 cm. b0 on T1-01 is still -0.85/-0.56 cm.
- What remains is an x (reach) SHORTFALL that does not depend on pick side. Every block sampled ends at x = 0.827-0.837 against a target of 0.85, so dx is about -1.3 to -2.3 cm, typically -1.5. In v0 R-027 it was about -2.0 cm. This shortfall is now almost the whole error.
- THE CORRECTION DOES NOT CONVERGE. pre_release_err_m is 1.4-2.3 cm in every stage sampled, against a pre-registered convergence target of <= 0.3 cm. Each correction pass commands +1.1 to +2.0 cm in x with an IK residual of about 1e-6 m, yet remaining_err_m stays flat or GROWS. Examples: T1-01 b0 2.49 -> 2.21 -> 2.21 cm; T3-06 b0 1.04 -> 1.83 cm. The y component of the command is realized; the x and z components are not. Pre-release z is also about 6-7 mm BELOW the seat height (e.g. 0.0431-0.0442 vs 0.05 for floor blocks), while v0 is about 2 mm below. Candidate causes, not yet tested: (a) the block is already pressed into its support/floor at the place waypoint, so contact friction resists x motion and the welded block doesn't follow the tip; (b) the joint controller doesn't track the IK solution under load (gravity sag at reach). In both, IK says solved but the block doesn't move. A frame or sign error on x/z in closed_loop_place.py is also possible and is cheap to rule out.

Hypothesis verdict: H-006 is REFUTED by its own pre-registered criteria. The median error is >= 1.5 cm on every tier, and pre-release error is not small, so this is NOT the 'release/settle' branch (H-005). The residual sits in arm positioning along x/z, which the correction fails to actuate. The narrower sub-claim (closed-loop correction removes the pick-side LATERAL bias) is supported on the sampled T1 scenes. Any gain holds only with a pose oracle.

IDEALIZATIONS STILL IN USE (v4_place): oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, oracle_block_pose (NEW: the correction reads the true block pose from the sim; no camera, depth or estimator), fixed_base, weld_grasp, no_arm_block_collision. v4_place DROPS scripted_arm in favor of closed-loop IK correction. The v0 control still uses scripted_arm, so the arms differ by two idealizations at once. Implicit: yaw=0 and x=0 scene construction, grid-aligned T2/T3 targets, and determinism-based reuse of controls across commits cbd9b16 and 911b67d+dirty.
  - failures: v4_place has 1 failure in 136 episodes: T3-dev-06 seed 1, placement_error on block b3 (second layer, target y=-0.065, z=0.15). Final pos_err is 5.8 cm, ang_err 91.7 deg, and final z is 0.176, so the block ended up on its side and too high.
Category: GRASP-ORIENTATION ERROR, inherited from the scripted pick, not caused by the correction. The log shows b3's pre_release quaternion is already about 90 deg about y (w=0.70, y=0.71) before release. The correction acts only on translation, so it cannot fix this. Codex analyze_rollout on the contact sheet (runs/1791079632_v4_place/rollout_T3-dev-06_s1.png) says: 'phase: carry; b3 is already held about 90 deg off its target orientation during carry and remains misoriented into placement. No visible toppling on release, collision with a neighbor, or correction-induced disturbance.'
This same scene and seed also FAILED in v0 (R-012, T3-06 s1, placement_error, last block 3.3 cm). It is a seed-specific failure that both arms share, not a regression. (In v0 the failing block differs, b5 at 3.3 cm, so the mechanism may differ between arms. The paired outcome is still fail/fail.)
v0 failures at seeds 0-3 that v4 fixed: T2-dev-07 s0 (b2 3.5 cm -> 2.7 cm) and T3-dev-03 s2 (3.4 cm -> 1.9 cm). Both are marginal over-tolerance placements that the ~0.5 cm improvement pulled back under 3 cm. T3-03 s2 in v4 still has two blocks at 2.6-2.7 cm, so the margin is thin.
Near misses in v4 (any block >= 2.5 cm): T2-07 (2.4-2.7), T3-03 s2 (2.6, 2.7), T3-06 (2.4-2.5), T2-01 (2.5). The 3 cm budget is still mostly consumed by the x shortfall.
Categories: grasp orientation 1; correction-induced disturbance 0; release topple 0; lateral bias 0.
  - review: accept by reviewer: ACCEPT. The comparison is matched on the same 34 T1-T3 dev scenes and seeds 0-3: R-028 supplies all 136 v4_place episodes; the v0 rows are the seed-0..3 subsets of R-005/R-007/R-012/R-017, whose scene unions exactly cover those 34 scenes. Those control rows give T1 48/48, T2 39/40, T3 46/48, pooled 133/136, matching RS-010. Recomputing R-028 gives 135/136, Wilson 95% CI [0.9595,0.9987], with tier counts 48/48, 40/40, 47/48, exactly as reported. The control and treatment CIs overlap and the paired discordance is only 2 improvements versus 0 regressions (exact McNemar p=0.5), so RS-010 correctly calls the success difference unresolved rather than claiming superiority. Scene-level effective n is explicitly stated (10-12 scenes/tier), with all-four-seeds scene counts and Wilson intervals reported; those lower bounds are below 0.8. Only dev scenes occur in H-006, R-027, R-028, and the reused controls; no held-out/final-evaluation scene was used. H-006 was created at 1791076339.9, before R-027 at 1791079605.5 and R-028 at 1791079991.8, so the pre-registration label is supported by timestamps. H-006 is properly marked refuted despite 135/136 episode success because its own disjunctive refutation rule is met: the reported per-tier median position errors are >=1.5 cm, and independently the scene-level Wilson lower bounds are <0.8. The result fully lists remaining idealizations and explicitly discloses that v4_place both adds oracle_block_pose and drops scripted_arm relative to v0, making the interpretation conditional on a pose oracle and a two-idealization arm change. Finally, the pose decomposition is clearly labeled as a hand-read sample of six episodes plus the failure; statements about x/lateral behavior are bounded to sampled blocks/scenes, causal explanations are labeled candidates not yet tested, and the narrower lateral claim is limited to sampled T1 scenes.
  - surprise: 1) The correction loop does not close. With an IK residual of about 1e-6 m, the commanded +1-2 cm x correction is not realized by the block; remaining error stays flat or grows across passes (e.g. T3-06 b0 1.04 -> 1.83 cm). The improvement comes almost entirely from y. 2) Once the lateral pick-side bias is removed, a pick-side-INDEPENDENT x shortfall of about -1.5 cm is exposed: every block lands short of x=0.85. RS-001/RS-002 had attributed the whole ~2 cm v0 offset to lateral bias, so that decomposition was incomplete. 3) Pre-release z is 6-7 mm below seat height in v4 (about 2 mm in v0). The correction drives the block into its support, which plausibly blocks the x motion through contact friction. 4) The only v4 failure is a 90 deg grasp-orientation error on a seed that v0 also fails. Translation-only correction is blind to it.
- **RS-011** runs R-029: 1.00 [0.21, 1.00] (n=1); R-030: 0.80 [0.58, 0.92] (n=20); R-031: 1.00 [0.44, 1.00] (n=3); R-032: 1.00 [0.21, 1.00] (n=1); R-003: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48)
  - interpretation: Zero new episodes. The R-030 T0-dev-02 s1 re-render was diagnostic only and is not counted.

DESIGN / TIMESTAMPS: pre-registered treatment with a reused historical control.
- H-009 was created at 1791077117.98 and its binding PI rulings were added at 1791077472.22. The earliest X-019 run, R-029, was created at 1791080136.89 (run dir 1791080123, first Sumo id 1791080125). So H-009 precedes the data by ~3019 s (~50 min) and the rulings by ~2665 s (~44 min). Pre-registration is CONFIRMED.
- Control: R-003 (v0, commit cbd9b16, 1791061357), seed 0-3 rows on T0-dev-01..06, giving 24 pairs by (scene, seed).

R-032 (T0-dev-01 s0) reproduces R-029 bit-exactly: block 2.0827 cm / 2.4753 deg; base 1.64 cm / 15.87 deg; identical final_qpos. So the working-tree change did not alter that uncontended episode. It does NOT show determinism under load (see SURPRISE).

SUCCESS (v2_walk pooled R-029+R-030+R-031): 20/24 = 0.833, Wilson 95% [0.641, 0.933].
- Control R-003 seeds 0-3: 24/24, Wilson [0.862, 1.000].
