PLACEMENT ERROR, paired v2 minus v0 (cm, from full-precision episodes.jsonl), on the 20 successful pairs:
- Median -0.126 cm. Order-statistic 95% CI (ranks 6-15) [-0.207, +0.066] cm.
- Range [-1.27 (T0-dev-06 s0), +0.37 (T0-dev-03 s0)].
- 7 of 20 pairs increased, 13 decreased.
- Over all 24 pairs (failures count as +44 to +48 cm), the median is -0.068 cm.
- Max block error among successes is 2.60 cm (T0-dev-04 s3), within the 2.7 cm limit. Including failures it is 50.5 cm.
- Pooled median block error is 2.26 cm vs 2.35 cm for v0.
- So whenever the arm can reach, walking adds no measurable placement error. The arm re-plans from the true base pose and compensates (e.g. T0-dev-04 s1: base off 3.01 cm, block 2.44 cm vs v0 2.47 cm).

BASE POSE vs OUTCOME (Sumo terminal pose vs nominal goal (-0.15, 0, 0.52), yaw 0):
- Position error:
  - Successes: median 1.49 cm, range 0.29-3.01 cm.
  - Failures: 0.81, 1.59, 1.47, 0.24 cm (median 1.14 cm).
  - Mann-Whitney U_fail = 27 of 80 (expected 40). Failures do NOT have larger position error.
- Yaw error:
  - Successes: median 20.8 deg, range 9.8-68.2 deg.
  - Failures: 90.65 (T0-dev-02 s1), 77.08 (s2), 179.22 (s3), 98.59 (T0-dev-04 s2), median 94.6 deg.
  - Complete separation: every failure exceeds every success (U = 80/80). Exact one-sided p = 1/C(24,4) = 9.4e-5. Caveat: episodes are not independent, since the same terminal pose recurs.
- Empirically, failures start between 68 and 77 deg of yaw error.
- Approach position error exceeds 1 cm in 13/24 episodes, so the AT RISK approach clause would also fire.
- The yaw error is large in nearly every episode: the Sumo stage reports success=true even at 179 deg, so its success gate ignores heading.

BRIDGE AUDIT (sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge): in all 25 episodes, assembly_base_pose = sumo_terminal_base_pose + (+0.15, 0, -0.06) m, with the quaternion passed unchanged. The bridge does NOT snap the base to the goal: the true walked pose, including yaw, reaches the assembly sim. The failures are therefore real consequences of the walked heading, not a bridge artifact. The constant offset itself is an idealization (frames are aligned by a fixed hand-set transform, not estimated).

H-009 RULE (order: NOT CLEARED, then AT RISK, then CLEARED): NOT CLEARED fires on 4 failures (and blocks > 3 cm). The method_designer's prediction "CLEARED" is REFUTED. Rung 2 is not cleared on T0.

IDEALIZATIONS STILL IN USE: oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision, sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge. The bridge includes the fixed (+0.15, 0, -0.06) m Sumo-to-assembly frame offset, assumed exact, with no base-pose estimation. The base pose is also oracle (the true walked pose is handed to the arm planner; there is no localization).
  - failures: All four failures are 'unreachable' (category count 4/4). Episodes: T0-dev-02 s1, s2, s3 and T0-dev-04 s2.
- Each ended at sim time 5.0 s with pre_release_pose = null: no grasp and no transport.
- The block is left near its start position, (0.43-0.51, 0.21-0.37), which is 46.5-50.5 cm from the target (0.85, 0).
- Mechanism: the walk finished with base heading 77-179 deg off nominal, at good xy (0.24-1.59 cm). The pick and/or target then fall outside the scripted arm's reachable workspace, and the planner aborts.

Visual check, codex on the ORIGINAL runs/1791080164_v2_walk/T0-dev-02_s1.gif:
- phase = approach. The base finishes about 90 deg off, side-on to the block and target. The arm stays stowed with no reach attempt, and there is no collision or drop.
- This matches the logged yaw of 90.65 deg.

Re-render (render_rollout R-030 T0-dev-02 s1, runs/1791080164_v2_walk/rollout_T0-dev-02_s1.gif):
- The SAME episode SUCCEEDED: walk yaw 2.26 deg, base 0.85 cm, block 2.04 cm.
- Codex confirms the base is square to the target with a clean reach and placement.

No placement_error, drop, slip or collapse failures occurred. Every successful episode stays within 2.60 cm.
  - review: reject by reviewer: REJECT. The matched-control design is present: X-019/R-029..R-031 cover T0-dev-01..06 seeds 0-3 (24 episodes), and R-003 contains the same scene/seed pairs; held-out scenes remain policy-locked and none appear in these runs. H-009 (created 1791077117.98) and its binding rule clarification (1791077472.22) predate R-029 (1791080136.89), so pre-registration is confirmed. Recomputed pooled treatment metrics match RS-011: 20/24 success, Wilson 95% CI [0.6415,0.9332], median position error 2.2633 cm, four unreachable failures; R-003's recorded full-run metrics also recompute. RS-011 appropriately says the treatment-control success difference is statistically unresolved, while H-009 is nevertheless refuted under its explicitly pre-registered NOT CLEARED rule ('any failure'). The yaw/outcome statement is acceptable only as an empirical association: the reported complete split is paired with the necessary non-independence caveat and should not be generalized beyond these scheduled episodes. The decisive defect is that the stronger load-dependence/concurrency interpretation is not adequately evidenced: re-simulating only T0-dev-02 s1 successfully at 2.3 deg demonstrates one episode's non-reproducibility, not that CPU load caused the change or that a serial rerun would clear. Wall-time/failure correlation is observational and confounded; no controlled repeated load-vs-serial runs or replication across failed pairs exists. Mixed dirty working-tree fingerprints (R-029/R-030, R-031, R-032) further limit attribution; one bit-exact uncontended repeat does not validate equivalence across all pooled episodes. The result does disclose those fingerprints and lists the continuing idealizations, but its load-causal language ('failures track contention', 'failure rate depends on scheduling', 'only masked by low load') exceeds the evidence. Rephrase as a single-case reproducibility anomaly/hypothesis or add controlled replicated load tests.
  - surprise: 1) The Sumo walk is NOT reproducible per (scene, seed) under CPU contention, and the failures track contention.
- Re-simulating the failed R-030 T0-dev-02 s1 alone gave yaw 2.26 deg and SUCCESS. The original run gave 90.65 deg and failure; its walk stage took 26.4 s wall, vs 5.7 s for the re-render.
- The first wave (Sumo id 1791080171, walk wall 22-28 s) produced 3 of the 4 failures and the widest yaw spread. The 4th failure (T0-dev-04 s2) took 15.5 s.
- Every uncontended walk (wall 3.3-8.6 s) lands on one of two identical poses:
  - 15.87 deg / 1.64 cm: T0-dev-01 s0 (R-029 and R-032), T0-dev-04 s0, T0-dev-05 s2 and s3, T0-dev-06 s0 and s1.
  - 9.78 deg: T0-dev-06 s2 and s3.
- Identical outputs across different seeds mean the episode seed is apparently not passed to the Sumo walk. The observed variation comes from something else, most likely a wall-clock-coupled CEM/MPC budget, even though mpc_iterations_completed = 81 everywhere.
- This contradicts X-019's planning assumption that "results are deterministic, so contention costs only wall time" (shown in RS-004 only for v0 MuJoCo, and flagged as unverified for Sumo CEM in X-022/X-023).
- Consequence: the 20/24 rate is a property of v2_walk *as scheduled on 8 contended workers*, not a stable per-(scene, seed) property. A serial rerun could plausibly clear 24/24, and that would not be a fair fix: the heading failure mode is real and only masked by low load.

2) The Sumo spot_walk_approach 'success' flag ignores heading. It reports success at 179 deg yaw error. The approach needs a yaw gate (or the arm needs base re-orientation / a yaw-aware re-approach) before rung 2 can clear.

3) Given reach, walking slightly LOWERS block error vs v0 (paired median -0.13 cm), so the 3 cm T1 budget concern from X-019 is not driven by placement error. It is driven by reachability.
- **RS-012** runs R-033: 0.78 [0.62, 0.88] (n=40); R-034: 0.50 [0.33, 0.67] (n=28); R-035: 0.94 [0.72, 0.99] (n=16); R-016: 0.67 [0.53, 0.78] (n=48); R-005: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-017: 0.98 [0.93, 0.99] (n=128)
  - interpretation: PRE-REGISTRATION: H-008 was created at t=1791077053, 3,250 s before X-029 (t=1791080306) and about 3,430 s before R-033 started (dir 1791080487). R-034 and R-035 came later still. So H-008 predates every result here. But its stated prerequisite ('if H-006 is refuted, this test is moot') failed: the analyst refuted H-006 at t=1791080485 (RS-010), 2 s before R-033 launched. Everything below is read against H-008's own rule anyway.

SCENE-LEVEL RESULTS (a scene passes only if both seeds pass; Wilson 95% CIs). Seeds are matched at 0-1. Reused controls use only their seed 0-1 rows: R-016 = v5_vlm on T1-01..06, R-005/R-017 = v0 on T1.
- T1, 12 scenes: v45 12/12 [0.76, 1.00], 24/24 episodes [0.86, 1.00]. v5_vlm 8/12 [0.39, 0.86] (R-016 4/6 + R-034 4/6), 16/24 episodes. v0 12/12. Effect of v45 vs v5_vlm is +33 pp at scene level. The 4 discordant scenes (dev-02, dev-05, dev-08, dev-11) all favor v45. Exact McNemar p = 0.125, NOT RESOLVED at n = 12 scenes (< 20).
- Off-grid T2 (dev-11..14): v45 2/4 [0.15, 0.85], 5/8 episodes. v5_vlm 3/4 [0.30, 0.95], 6/8. v0 3/4 [0.30, 0.95], 7/8.
- Off-grid T3 (dev-13..16): v45 1/4 [0.05, 0.70], 2/8 episodes [0.07, 0.59]. v5_vlm 0/4 [0, 0.49], 0/8 [0, 0.32]. v0 4/4 [0.51, 1.00], 8/8 [0.68, 1.00].
- Off-grid pooled: v45 3/8 scenes [0.14, 0.69], 7/16 episodes [0.23, 0.67]. v0 7/8 scenes [0.53, 0.98], 15/16 episodes [0.72, 0.99]. That is -50 pp at scene level and -50 pp at episode level. Scenes: 4 discordant pairs, all favoring v0, McNemar p = 0.125. Episodes: 8 vs 0, p = 0.008, but episodes are seed-correlated, so the scene-level test is the honest one. NOT RESOLVED as a population rate, though the direction is consistent.

H-008 DECISION RULE:
(a) T1 6/6 on dev-01..06: MET, and 12/12 on the extended set.
(b) b1 error <= 2.7 cm on dev-02: MISSED, observed 2.97 / 3.0 cm.
(c) b1 error <= 1.8 cm on dev-05: MISSED, observed 2.06 / 2.0 cm.
(d) No regression on T2/T3 vs v5_vlm: MISSED on T2. T2-dev-14 seed 0 fails under v45 (b1 3.06 cm) and passes under v5_vlm. T2 goes 6/8 -> 5/8 episodes and 3/4 -> 2/4 scenes, and 'any drop refutes'. T3 improves from 0/8 to 2/8.
No SUPPORTED or PARTIAL branch applies. Under its own rule, H-008 is REFUTED. The outcome (T1 gap closed on 12 dev scenes) holds. The mechanism and the no-regression clause do not.

'b1 ERROR CLOSE TO |e|' CHECK, from the per-episode pose fields. The 2nd correction pass commands about 0 in y, so I use pre_release_y as the VLM-parsed lateral target. That gives e = true_y - pre_release_y, and placement = final - VLM target.
- dev-01 b1: e = 2.5 cm, dx = -1.56 cm, total 2.84.
- dev-02 b1: e = 2.4 cm, dx = -1.50 cm, total 2.97.
- dev-05 b1: e = 1.2 cm, dx = -1.59 cm, total 2.06.
So the error is not |e| + drift. It is |e| (+) 1.5 cm in quadrature. The residual is a pick-side-independent x shortfall of about 1.5 cm, the same one RS-010 found. Correction passes never shrink remaining_err (about 1.8-2.2 cm before and after, IK residual about 1e-6). closed_loop_weld_place does not remove b. It swaps the lateral pick-side bias for an x shortfall that is ORTHOGONAL to the lateral VLM error. That is why T1 passes: sqrt(e^2 + 1.5^2) stays under 3 cm for e <= 2.6 cm, whereas e + b used to add linearly.

OFF-GRID FAILURE DECOMPOSITION (worst block; perception = lateral VLM quantization vs true target, placement = final vs VLM target):
- T2-dev-11 s0/s1: b2 true y 4.0, VLM 0.0 -> perception 3.96, placement 1.43 (x), total 4.10. PERCEPTION ALONE > 3 cm. The VLM read a 2.2 / 1.8 cm per-level staircase as a vertical stack: each relative offset sits below half a grid step, so it snaps to 0 and the error accumulates.
- T3-dev-14 s0/s1: b2 true 4.5, VLM 0.0 -> perception 4.47, placement 1.45, total 4.56. PERCEPTION ALONE > 3 cm. Same collapse to a vertical stack.
- T3-dev-15 s0/s1: b5 true 2.8, VLM 0.0 -> perception 2.85, placement 1.42, total 3.31. MARGINAL: perception is under 3 cm and the x shortfall pushes it over.
- T2-dev-14 s0: b1 true -2.7, VLM 0.0 -> perception 2.7, placement 1.55, total 3.06. MARGINAL, same pattern. Seed 1 passed (b1 1.6 cm) because the VLM output differed.
- T3-dev-16 s1: b5 true -1.9, VLM -6.3 -> perception 4.4 (snapped one grid step the wrong way), total 4.75. PERCEPTION.
- T3-dev-16 s0: perception_mismatch + missing_block. b4 was never placed (it stayed on the table 52.6 cm away), and b5 landed on level 2 instead of level 3 (10.3 cm). Structural mis-parse.
Summary: 5/9 failures are perception-dominated (e > 3 cm, or a structural mis-parse), 4/9 are marginal (e between 2.7 and 2.9 cm plus the 1.5 cm x shortfall), and 0/9 are placement-only. In H-008's own terms, the off-grid failures have b-error approximately |e|, so the next step is continuous lateral perception, not more placement work.

Idealizations still in use: oracle_build_order, oracle_block_pose (the correction reads sim truth), fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision; implicitly also yaw = 0, x = 0, clean rendered images. v0 additionally has oracle_structure_spec. Dev scenes only, nothing held out.
  - failures: v45 R-033: 9 failed episodes out of 40, all on off-grid T2/T3. Logged categories: placement_error 8, perception_mismatch 1 + perception_missing_block 1. Re-categorized by error source (perception = |VLM lateral target - true target|, placement = |final - VLM target|, which is about 1.4-1.6 cm of x shortfall in every block):
(1) PERCEPTION, staircase collapsed to a vertical stack: e = 4.0-4.5 cm > tolerance by itself. T2-dev-11 s0/s1, T3-dev-14 s0/s1. 4 episodes.
(2) PERCEPTION, snapped to the wrong grid cell: e = 4.4 cm. T3-dev-16 s1. 1 episode.
(3) PERCEPTION, structural mis-parse: a block was never placed and another went to the wrong level. T3-dev-16 s0. 1 episode.
(4) MARGINAL, quadrature sum: e = 2.7-2.85 cm combined with the 1.5 cm x shortfall gives 3.06-3.31 cm. T3-dev-15 s0/s1, T2-dev-14 s0. 3 episodes. Fixing the x shortfall alone would rescue these 3, giving 10/16 off-grid, still below v0's 15/16.
VISUAL CHECK of T3-dev-14 s0 (render_rollout plus codex on prompts/analyze_rollout/v1.md): 'final structure is essentially a vertical stack: b2 placed nearly centered over b1 instead of with the required staircase overhang. No knock-over, early drop, collapse or arm collision.' This confirms category (1).
v5_vlm R-034: 14 failed episodes out of 28. Off-grid T3 is 0/8 (perception plus additive pick-side bias). T1-dev-08 and T1-dev-11 fail at 4.5 / 3.5 cm. v0 R-035: 1 failure out of 16, T2-dev-11 s0, a 12.2 / 8.6 cm disturbance (one seed only).
  - review: reject by reviewer: REJECT. Decisive chronology: R-033's episodes.jsonl was completed at epoch 1791080806 (04:26:46); the untracked lab/stages/closed_loop_place.py was modified at 1791081259 (04:34:19) to accumulate the measured block miss into the next commanded target; rollout_T3-dev-14_s0.{png,gif} was created at 1791081311-1313 (04:35:11-13). The re-sim therefore used post-fix code. Its 0.11 cm convergence versus the logged R-033 non-convergence is explained by a changed controller and provides no evidence of run nondeterminism or an environment difference; surprise (2) must withdraw that reading, and it cannot support reopening H-006. The matched-control claim is also compromised for the VLM comparison: R-033 has 16/40 perceive.cached=false episodes, while R-034 is 28/28 cached=true. In the cited T2-dev-14 s0 regression, v45's uncached draw differs from the cached v5_vlm spec, so the failure is not a clean build-stage comparison on a shared perception output. The data support seed-to-seed variation for uncached v45 invocations (e.g. T2-dev-14 and T3-dev-16), but not the broader statement that seed invariance breaks for 'VLM methods': every R-034 v5_vlm episode is cached, and the variation is confounded with uncached stochastic sampling/run order rather than established as seed-driven. Pre-registration is overstated: H-008 (1791077053.84) precedes new R-033/R-034/R-035, but reused controls R-005 (1791061396.08), R-016 (1791070570.86), and R-017 (1791070753.93) all predate H-008; thus it does not 'predate every result here.' Moreover H-008 explicitly says the test is moot if H-006 is refuted, and H-006 was refuted before R-033 ran. The numerical run summaries themselves recompute exactly; all scenes are dev/final_eval=false; idealizations are listed; and RS-012 correctly treats scene-level McNemar p=0.125 and overlapping scene-level CIs as unresolved rather than claiming a population-rate difference. Those positives do not cure the invalid post-fix re-sim interpretation and non-identical VLM control pairing.
  - surprise: (1) The T1 gap closed for the wrong reason. closed_loop_weld_place does not remove the bias b. It turns it into a constant x shortfall of about 1.5 cm that is orthogonal to the VLM's lateral error, so the two add in quadrature instead of linearly. The b1 errors miss H-008's predictions: 2.97 vs <= 2.7 on dev-02 and 2.06 vs <= 1.8 on dev-05.
(2) render_rollout of T3-dev-14 s0 does NOT reproduce the logged stages. In the re-sim the correction converged (pre_release_err 0.11 / 0.11 cm on b1/b2, final x about 0.847), while the logged run never converged (remaining_err about 1.8-1.9 cm). Same perceive key. The x-shortfall that RS-010 attributed to the stage may come from run nondeterminism or an environment/version difference (commit is +dirty), not from the controller. The episode still fails in the re-sim, at 4.3 cm, on perception alone.
(3) The seed-invariance assumption breaks for v45/v5_vlm on new scenes. perceive.cached = false, so the VLM is sampled once per seed and the outputs differ: T2-dev-14 seed 0 VLM gives b1 y = 0 (fail), seed 1 gives about -2.5 (pass), and T3-dev-16 s0 vs s1 parse different structures. For VLM methods the effective n is per-seed again, and X-029's 'effective n = scenes' premise does not hold.
(4) On off-grid T3, v45 is worse than oracle-spec v0 by 6/8 episodes. The rung-5 gap has moved from T1 to off-grid structures and is now perception-limited.
- **RS-013** runs R-029: 1.00 [0.21, 1.00] (n=1); R-030: 0.80 [0.58, 0.92] (n=20); R-031: 1.00 [0.44, 1.00] (n=3); R-032: 1.00 [0.21, 1.00] (n=1); R-003: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48)
  - interpretation: SUPERSEDES RS-011. The reviewer rejected RS-011 because its claim that CPU contention caused the failures went beyond the evidence. This revision has the same runs, numbers and verdict. Only the causal language has changed: here and in the SURPRISE section, contention is stated as a hypothesis that has not been tested.

Zero new episodes. The R-030 T0-dev-02 s1 re-render was diagnostic only and is not counted.

DESIGN / TIMESTAMPS: pre-registered treatment with a reused historical control.
- H-009 was created at 1791077117.98 and its binding PI rulings were added at 1791077472.22. The earliest X-019 run, R-029, was created at 1791080136.89 (run dir 1791080123, first Sumo id 1791080125). So H-009 precedes the data by ~3019 s (~50 min) and the rulings by ~2665 s (~44 min). Pre-registration is CONFIRMED.
- Control: R-003 (v0, commit cbd9b16, 1791061357), seed 0-3 rows on T0-dev-01..06, giving 24 pairs by (scene, seed).
- Code fingerprints are mixed. R-029 and R-030 are 911b67d+dirty.7568258f, R-031 is dirty.effda19e, and R-032 is dirty.2e7a4acb.
  - R-032 (T0-dev-01 s0) reproduces R-029 bit-exactly: block 2.0827 cm / 2.4753 deg; base 1.64 cm / 15.87 deg; identical final_qpos.
  - For that one episode, the working-tree change did not alter the outcome.
  - It does NOT show that the other pooled episodes are equivalent across fingerprints, and it does not show that episodes are reproducible in general (see SURPRISE).

SUCCESS (v2_walk pooled R-029+R-030+R-031): 20/24 = 0.833, Wilson 95% [0.641, 0.933].
- Control R-003 seeds 0-3: 24/24, Wilson [0.862, 1.000].
- Difference: -16.7 pp, Newcombe 95% CI about [-0.36, +0.00].
- Paired: 4 discordant pairs, all v0-pass / v2-fail. Exact McNemar p = 0.125.
- With n = 24 the success difference is NOT statistically resolved. The pre-registered rule, however, is deterministic on "any failure".

Scene-level (n = 4 each, Wilson):
| scene | successes | Wilson 95% |
|---|---|---|

PLACEMENT ERROR, paired v2 minus v0 (cm, from full-precision episodes.jsonl), on the 20 successful pairs:
- Median -0.126 cm. Order-statistic 95% CI (ranks 6-15) [-0.207, +0.066] cm.
- Range [-1.27 (T0-dev-06 s0), +0.37 (T0-dev-03 s0)].
- 7 of 20 pairs increased, 13 decreased.
- Over all 24 pairs (failures count as +44 to +48 cm), the median is -0.068 cm.
- Max block error among successes is 2.60 cm (T0-dev-04 s3), within the 2.7 cm limit. Including failures it is 50.5 cm.
- Pooled median block error is 2.26 cm vs 2.35 cm for v0.
- So whenever the arm can reach, walking adds no measurable placement error. The arm re-plans from the true base pose and compensates (e.g. T0-dev-04 s1: base off 3.01 cm, block 2.44 cm vs v0 2.47 cm).

BASE POSE vs OUTCOME (Sumo terminal pose vs nominal goal (-0.15, 0, 0.52), yaw 0):
- Position error:
  - Successes: median 1.49 cm, range 0.29-3.01 cm.
  - Failures: 0.81, 1.59, 1.47, 0.24 cm (median 1.14 cm).
  - Mann-Whitney U_fail = 27 of 80 (expected 40). Failures do NOT have larger position error.
- Yaw error:
  - Successes: median 20.8 deg, range 9.8-68.2 deg.
  - Failures: 90.65 (T0-dev-02 s1), 77.08 (s2), 179.22 (s3), 98.59 (T0-dev-04 s2), median 94.6 deg.
  - Complete separation: every failure exceeds every success (U = 80/80). Exact one-sided p = 1/C(24,4) = 9.4e-5.
  - Caveat: this is an empirical association within these scheduled episodes. Episodes are not independent, since the same terminal pose recurs, so it should not be generalized.
- In these episodes, failures start between 68 and 77 deg of yaw error.
- Approach position error exceeds 1 cm in 13/24 episodes, so the AT RISK approach clause would also fire.
- The yaw error is large in nearly every episode: the Sumo stage reports success=true even at 179 deg, so its success gate ignores heading.

BRIDGE AUDIT (sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge): in all 25 episodes, assembly_base_pose = sumo_terminal_base_pose + (+0.15, 0, -0.06) m, with the quaternion passed unchanged. The bridge does NOT snap the base to the goal: the true walked pose, including yaw, reaches the assembly sim. The failures are therefore real consequences of the walked heading, not a bridge artifact. The constant offset itself is an idealization (frames are aligned by a fixed hand-set transform, not estimated).

H-009 RULE (order: NOT CLEARED, then AT RISK, then CLEARED): NOT CLEARED fires on 4 failures (and blocks > 3 cm). The method_designer's prediction "CLEARED" is REFUTED. Rung 2 is not cleared on T0.

IDEALIZATIONS STILL IN USE: oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision, sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge. The bridge includes the fixed (+0.15, 0, -0.06) m Sumo-to-assembly frame offset, assumed exact, with no base-pose estimation. The base pose is also oracle (the true walked pose is handed to the arm planner; there is no localization).
  - failures: All four failures are 'unreachable' (category count 4/4). Episodes: T0-dev-02 s1, s2, s3 and T0-dev-04 s2.
- Each ended at sim time 5.0 s with pre_release_pose = null: no grasp and no transport.
- The block is left near its start position, (0.43-0.51, 0.21-0.37), which is 46.5-50.5 cm from the target (0.85, 0).
- Mechanism: the walk finished with base heading 77-179 deg off nominal, at good xy (0.24-1.59 cm). The pick and/or target then fall outside the scripted arm's reachable workspace, and the planner aborts.

Visual check, codex on the ORIGINAL runs/1791080164_v2_walk/T0-dev-02_s1.gif:
- phase = approach. The base finishes about 90 deg off, side-on to the block and target. The arm stays stowed with no reach attempt, and there is no collision or drop.
- This matches the logged yaw of 90.65 deg.

Re-render (render_rollout R-030 T0-dev-02 s1, runs/1791080164_v2_walk/rollout_T0-dev-02_s1.gif):
- The SAME episode SUCCEEDED: walk yaw 2.26 deg, base 0.85 cm, block 2.04 cm.
- Codex confirms the base is square to the target with a clean reach and placement.

No placement_error, drop, slip or collapse failures occurred. Every successful episode stays within 2.60 cm.
  - review: accept by reviewer: ACCEPT. RS-013 uses the same records as RS-011: treatment R-029..R-031 covers T0-dev-01..06 at seeds 0-3 (24 episodes), R-032 is the same single diagnostic repeat, and historical control R-003 contains the same six scenes and seeds; all are development scenes and final_eval=false, with no held-out scene use. Recomputed treatment metrics are unchanged and match the claim: 20/24 success, Wilson 95% CI [0.6415, 0.9332], with four unreachable failures. R-003 remains 48/48 overall, so its matched seeds 0-3 are 24/24 (Wilson [0.862, 1.000]). The treatment-control difference remains explicitly statistically unresolved, while H-009 is refuted only under its pre-registered deterministic any-failure rule. The prior decisive defect is corrected: RS-013 limits demonstrated non-reproducibility to one re-simulated episode, labels CPU contention only a candidate cause based on confounded observational evidence, labels the wall-clock-limited CEM explanation an untested code-reading hypothesis, and explicitly disclaims causation, scheduling dependence, and serial clearance. Metrics, runs, verdict, failure analysis, disclosures, and listed idealizations are unchanged; the other wording changes are non-substantive caveat/clarity edits consistent with narrowing the same claim.
  - surprise: 1) Not reproducible per (scene, seed). So far this is demonstrated for ONE episode only.
- Re-simulating the failed R-030 T0-dev-02 s1 alone gave yaw 2.26 deg and SUCCESS. The original gave 90.65 deg and failure.
- This is a single-case reproducibility anomaly. No other failed pair has been re-run, so it is not yet known how many of the 24 outcomes would change on a repeat.

2) CPU contention is a CANDIDATE cause, not an established one. The evidence is correlational only:
- The original walk stage took 26.4 s wall, vs 5.7 s for the solo re-render.
- The first wave (Sumo id 1791080171, walk wall 22-28 s) holds 3 of the 4 failures and the widest yaw spread. The 4th failure (T0-dev-04 s2) took 15.5 s.
- Uncontended walks (wall 3.3-8.6 s) landed on one of two recurring poses: 15.87 deg / 1.64 cm, or 9.78 deg.
- This evidence is observational and confounded:
  - wave membership is tied to scene/seed order;
  - the code fingerprints are mixed (dirty.7568258f / effda19e / 2e7a4acb);
  - there is no controlled load-vs-serial comparison.
- Mechanism hypothesis (engineer's code reading, NOT tested): the CEM rollouts inside the Sumo walk are wall-clock-limited. Under load, fewer or truncated rollouts per MPC step could change the plan even though mpc_iterations_completed = 81 everywhere.
- Until it is tested, we cannot say that contention caused the failures, that the failure rate depends on scheduling, or that a serial rerun would clear.
- RS-004's determinism finding covered v0 MuJoCo only and does not transfer to Sumo CEM, which X-022/X-023 already flagged as unverified.

3) The test is running now: experiment X-032 repeats T0-dev-02 s1 twice serially with max_workers=1.
- Two matching near-zero-yaw successes would show serial repeatability for this episode. They still would not show that contention caused the original failure, which would need a matched contended repeat.
- Disagreement between the two would point to non-determinism that does not depend on load.

4) At the time of X-019 the Sumo walk ignored the episode seed. Different seeds produced bit-identical terminal poses:
- 15.87 deg / 1.64 cm on T0-dev-01 s0 (R-029 and R-032), T0-dev-04 s0, T0-dev-05 s2 and s3, T0-dev-06 s0 and s1;
- 9.78 deg on T0-dev-06 s2 and s3.
- So seed-to-seed variation inside the walk came from something other than the seed.

5) The Sumo spot_walk_approach 'success' flag ignores heading. It reports success at 179 deg yaw error. The approach needs a yaw gate (or the arm needs base re-orientation / a yaw-aware re-approach) before rung 2 can clear. This holds regardless of the cause in (2): the 77-179 deg heading outcomes are a real failure mode of the approach as it stands.

6) Given reach, walking slightly LOWERS block error vs v0 (paired median -0.13 cm, CI [-0.21, +0.07]), so the 3 cm T1 budget concern from X-019 is not driven by placement error. It is driven by reachability.
- **RS-014** runs R-033: 0.78 [0.62, 0.88] (n=40); R-034: 0.50 [0.33, 0.67] (n=28); R-035: 0.94 [0.72, 0.99] (n=16); R-016: 0.67 [0.53, 0.78] (n=48); R-005: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-017: 0.98 [0.93, 0.99] (n=128)
  - interpretation: SUPERSEDES RS-012 (rejected by reviewer). Same runs, same metrics. Changes: (i) surprise (2) withdrawn, (ii) perception pairing re-stated per episode from the perceive.cached flags, (iii) H-008 re-evaluated without clause (d), (iv) pre-registration claim corrected.

PRE-REGISTRATION (corrected): H-008 (t=1791077053.8) predates the new runs R-033/R-034/R-035. It does NOT predate the reused controls R-005 (1791061396), R-016 (1791070571) and R-017 (1791070754). Its own prerequisite ('moot if H-006 is refuted') also failed: H-006 was refuted (RS-010) 2 s before R-033 launched. Everything below reads H-008 against its stated rule anyway, as a mechanism check, not as a clean pre-registered test.

PERCEPTION PAIRING, per episode (read from runs/1791080487_v45_place_vlm/episodes.jsonl, perceive.cached):
- R-033 CACHED (24/40): every T1 episode, T1-dev-01..12 s0/s1.
- R-033 UNCACHED (16/40): every off-grid episode. T2-dev-11, -12, -13, -14 s0/s1 and T3-dev-13, -14, -15, -16 s0/s1. Each was a fresh VLM sample with the same model_key as the matching control scene.
- R-034 v5_vlm: 28/28 cached. R-016 v5_vlm: cached.
- Cache keys match across arms: dev-02 = 9b480f2d... in R-033 and R-016, dev-05 = 5bdfe9e6... in both, T2-dev-14 = 76081900... in R-033 and R-034.
Consequences:
(1) T1, v45 vs v5_vlm: paired on perception. Both arms read the identical cached VLM output per scene, so on T1 the comparison isolates the build stage, and seeds differ only in sim noise.
(2) Off-grid, v45 vs v5_vlm: NOT paired. v45 used 16 independent uncached draws (2 per scene). v5_vlm reused one cached draw per scene for both seeds. Off-grid differences between v45 and v5_vlm mix the build-stage effect with VLM sampling variation, so I do not attribute them to the controller.
(3) Seed invariance, narrowed: the data show that UNCACHED v45 calls varied between the two seeds of the same scene. T2-dev-14: s0 parsed b1 lateral about 0 (fail), s1 about -2.7 (pass). T3-dev-16: s0 and s1 parsed different structures (s0 dropped b4). Cached episodes (all T1 in both arms, all of R-034) are perception-identical across seeds by construction. Because the uncached draws also differ in call order and time, the variation is stochastic VLM sampling, not established as seed-driven. 'Effective n = scenes' holds for cached episodes only. For uncached off-grid v45 episodes the effective unit is the episode/draw.

SCENE-LEVEL RESULTS (unchanged; a scene passes only if both seeds pass; Wilson 95% CIs; seeds matched at 0-1; reused controls use only their seed 0-1 rows):
- T1, 12 scenes (perception-paired vs v5_vlm): v45 12/12 [0.76, 1.00], 24/24 episodes [0.86, 1.00]. v5_vlm 8/12 [0.39, 0.86], 16/24 episodes. v0 12/12. Effect: +33 pp at scene level. 4 discordant scenes (dev-02, -05, -08, -11), all favor v45. Exact McNemar p = 0.125. NOT RESOLVED (n = 12 < 20).
- Off-grid T2 (dev-11..14): v45 2/4 [0.15, 0.85], 5/8 episodes. v5_vlm 3/4 [0.30, 0.95], 6/8. v0 3/4 [0.30, 0.95], 7/8. v45 vs v5_vlm is confounded by perception (see above).
- Off-grid T3 (dev-13..16): v45 1/4 [0.05, 0.70], 2/8 [0.07, 0.59]. v5_vlm 0/4 [0, 0.49], 0/8 [0, 0.32]. v0 4/4 [0.51, 1.00], 8/8 [0.68, 1.00].
- Off-grid pooled, v45 vs v0 (oracle spec, no VLM, so this comparison is unaffected by the cache issue): 3/8 scenes [0.14, 0.69] vs 7/8 [0.53, 0.98], and 7/16 episodes [0.23, 0.67] vs 15/16 [0.72, 0.99]. That is -50 pp at both levels. 4 discordant scenes, all favor v0, McNemar p = 0.125. NOT RESOLVED as a population rate. Direction is consistent.

H-008 RE-EVALUATED WITHOUT CLAUSE (d):
(a) T1 6/6 on dev-01..06: MET (12/12 on the extended set).
(b) b1 error <= 2.7 cm on dev-02: MISSED, observed 2.97 / 2.97 cm (s0/s1).
(c) b1 error <= 1.8 cm on dev-05: MISSED, observed 2.06 / 2.04 cm.
Are (b) and (c) comparable to the prediction's basis? YES. H-008's predicted values (dev-02 about 2.3, dev-05 about 1.4 = |e| + drift) were derived in RS-002 from v5_vlm's cached VLM outputs. The R-033 dev-02 and dev-05 episodes are cached=true with the same model_keys as R-016 (9b480f2d..., 5bdfe9e6...). They therefore executed the identical VLM spec the prediction was built on. The VLM-target offsets I recover (dev-02 e about 2.4 cm, dev-05 about 1.2 cm) agree with RS-002's 2.33 / 1.37 within the precision of the two estimation methods. The misses therefore cannot be attributed to a different perception draw. They are build-stage residuals: an orthogonal x shortfall of about 1.5 cm adding in quadrature (see the decomposition below).
(d) WITHDRAWN as evidence. The T2-dev-14 s0 'regression' compares an uncached v45 draw (b1 parsed about 0) with v5_vlm's cached spec. It is confounded by different VLM draws and does not count as a regression. The off-grid no-regression question is unanswered for v45 vs v5_vlm.
Verdict: H-008 stays REFUTED on clauses (b) and (c) alone, both on perception-identical episodes. The T1 outcome (gap closed on 12 dev scenes) holds at n = 12, not resolved. The predicted mechanism (b removed, error -> |e| + drift) does not.

'b1 ERROR vs |e|' CHECK (unchanged; per-episode pose fields; pre_release_y is used as the VLM-parsed lateral target because the 2nd correction pass commands about 0 in y):
- dev-01 b1: e = 2.5 cm, dx = -1.56 cm, total 2.84.
- dev-02 b1: e = 2.4 cm, dx = -1.50 cm, total 2.97.
- dev-05 b1: e = 1.2 cm, dx = -1.59 cm, total 2.06.
Error = |e| (+) about 1.5 cm in quadrature, not |e| + drift. Correction passes never reduce remaining_err (about 1.8-2.2 cm before and after; IK residual about 1e-6) in R-033's code version. closed_loop_weld_place swaps the lateral pick-side bias for an x shortfall orthogonal to the VLM's lateral error. That is why T1 passes: sqrt(e^2 + 1.5^2) < 3 cm for e <= 2.6 cm.

OFF-GRID PERCEPTION vs PLACEMENT DECOMPOSITION (unchanged; R-033 worst block; perception = |VLM lateral target - true|, placement = |final - VLM target|). All of these episodes are UNCACHED single draws, so each row describes that draw, not a property of the scene:
- T2-dev-11 s0/s1: b2 true 4.0, VLM 0.0. Perception 3.96, placement 1.43 (x), total 4.10. PERCEPTION ALONE > 3 cm. Staircase (2.2/1.8 cm per level) read as a vertical stack.
- T3-dev-14 s0/s1: b2 true 4.5, VLM 0.0. Perception 4.47, placement 1.45, total 4.56. PERCEPTION ALONE.
- T3-dev-15 s0/s1: b5 true 2.8, VLM 0.0. Perception 2.85, placement 1.42, total 3.31. MARGINAL.
- T2-dev-14 s0: b1 true -2.7, VLM 0.0. Perception 2.7, placement 1.55, total 3.06. MARGINAL. The s1 draw parsed about -2.7 and passed (1.57 cm).
- T3-dev-16 s1: b5 true -1.9, VLM -6.3. Perception 4.4 (one grid step the wrong way), total 4.75. PERCEPTION.
- T3-dev-16 s0: structural mis-parse. b4 never placed (52.6 cm), b5 on level 2 instead of 3 (10.3 cm).
Summary: 5/9 failures perception-dominated, 4/9 marginal (e 2.7-2.85 cm plus the 1.5 cm x shortfall), 0/9 placement-only.

Idealizations still in use: oracle_build_order, oracle_block_pose (the correction reads sim truth), fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision; implicitly yaw = 0, x = 0, clean rendered images, and cached VLM outputs on T1 (one draw per scene, so T1 results do not sample VLM variability). v0 additionally has oracle_structure_spec. Dev scenes only (final_eval = false), nothing held out.
  - failures: v45 R-033: 9 failed episodes out of 40, all off-grid T2/T3, and all UNCACHED perception. Logged: placement_error 8, perception_mismatch 1 + perception_missing_block 1. Re-categorized by error source:
(1) PERCEPTION, staircase collapsed to a vertical stack (e = 4.0-4.5 cm > tolerance alone): T2-dev-11 s0/s1, T3-dev-14 s0/s1. 4 episodes. Both seeds of each scene drew the same collapse.
(2) PERCEPTION, snapped to the wrong grid cell (e = 4.4 cm): T3-dev-16 s1. 1 episode.
(3) PERCEPTION, structural mis-parse (block never placed, another on the wrong level): T3-dev-16 s0. 1 episode. A different parse from s1 of the same scene, which shows draw-to-draw variation for uncached calls.
(4) MARGINAL, quadrature sum (e 2.7-2.85 cm with the 1.5 cm x shortfall gives 3.06-3.31 cm): T3-dev-15 s0/s1, T2-dev-14 s0. 3 episodes. Removing the x shortfall alone would rescue these, giving 10/16 off-grid, still below v0's 15/16.
VISUAL CHECK, T3-dev-14 s0 (render_rollout + codex on prompts/analyze_rollout/v1.md): 'final structure is essentially a vertical stack: b2 placed nearly centered over b1 instead of with the required staircase overhang. No knock-over, early drop, collapse or arm collision.' This confirms category (1) on the final structure. CAVEAT: that re-render ran at 04:35:11-13, after closed_loop_place.py was changed at 04:34:19. Its stage logs therefore reflect post-fix code, and only its final-structure perception reading is used here.
v5_vlm R-034: 14 failures out of 28, all on cached perception. Off-grid T3 is 0/8 (perception plus additive pick-side bias). T1-dev-08 and -11 fail at 4.5 / 3.5 cm. Off-grid v45 vs v5_vlm failure differences are not attributable to the controller (different draws).
v0 R-035: 1 failure out of 16, T2-dev-11 s0, a 12.2 / 8.6 cm disturbance (one seed only).
  - review: accept by reviewer: Accepted. Recomputed metrics for R-033/R-034/R-035/R-016/R-005/R-017 exactly match RS-014. v0 controls cover the cited comparisons on the same dev scenes and seeds 0-1 (R-005/R-017 for T1; R-035 for off-grid); every run is final_eval=false and uses only dev scenes, so held-out data were untouched. Wilson CIs and scene-level n are reported, and the T1 and pooled off-grid rate differences are correctly labeled unresolved (McNemar p=0.125); uncached off-grid draws are separately caveated. Episode records confirm T1-dev-02 and T1-dev-05 are cached in R-033 and R-016 with identical keys 9b480f2d41054e60b0fc9be1 and 5bdfe9e673e8c47c9dc23626, with R-033 b1 errors 2.97/2.97 cm and 2.06/2.04 cm, so H-008's refutation rests on clauses (b) and (c) only. The T2-dev-14 regression and T3-dev-14 nondeterminism claims are explicitly withdrawn. Timestamp audit is correct: H-008 predates R-033/034/035 but not R-005/R-016/R-017, and RS-014 disclaims a clean pre-registered test. Remaining idealizations are listed, including cached VLM outputs on T1, and interpretation stays within the evidence.
  - surprise: (1) The T1 gap closed for a different reason than predicted. closed_loop_weld_place (R-033 code) does not remove b. It converts it into a constant x shortfall of about 1.5 cm, orthogonal to the lateral VLM error, so the two add in quadrature. Shown on perception-identical (cached, same model_key) dev-02/dev-05 episodes: b1 errors of 2.97 vs <= 2.7 and 2.06 vs <= 1.8.
(2) [REVISED; RS-012's version withdrawn] The T3-dev-14 s0 re-sim that converged (pre_release_err 0.11 cm) is NOT evidence of nondeterminism or an environment difference. R-033 finished at 04:26:46. closed_loop_place.py was changed at 04:34:19 (the measured miss is now accumulated into the next commanded target). The re-sim ran at 04:35:11-13, so it used post-fix code. The difference is fully explained by the controller change. Read correctly, it is a PREVIEW that the fix converges: one block pair (b1/b2), one episode, one scene. The re-sim still failed at 4.3 cm on perception. It does not bear on reopening H-006 and needs a proper post-fix run to count for anything.
(3) [NARROWED] Uncached v45 VLM calls varied between the two seeds of a scene (T2-dev-14: b1 about 0 vs about -2.7; T3-dev-16: different structures). Cached episodes, meaning all T1 and all of R-034, are perception-identical across seeds. So the effective n for uncached off-grid VLM runs is per draw, and off-grid v45 vs v5_vlm is unpaired. Fix for future runs: run both arms on the same cached draw, or sample k draws per scene in both arms.
(4) On off-grid T3, v45 is worse than oracle-spec v0 by 6/8 episodes (unaffected by caching, since v0 has no VLM). The rung-5 gap has moved from T1 to off-grid structures and is perception-limited.

VERDICT. The strict pre-registered order puts VOID first. VOID fires on provenance: the treatment and control commits differ (details below). On substance the outcome is NOT CLEARED via H-014 rule 2(e): walked XY was 3.50 cm (>3 cm) at T0-dev-01 seed 6 (R-053). That walk sits inside the same-commit subset and replays bit-for-bit at the current HEAD (analyst re-render), so the provenance problem cannot remove it. The heading part of the claim passes. The "no side effect on XY" mechanism is refuted.

SUCCESS (Wilson 95%). Treatment v2_walk: T0 16/16 = 1.00 [0.806, 1.00]. Out-of-sample T0, seeds {0, 3..15}: 14/14 = 1.00 [0.785, 1.00]. T1 4/4 = 1.00 [0.510, 1.00]. All 20 episodes: 20/20 [0.839, 1.00]. Matched v0: 20/20 [0.839, 1.00]; T0 16/16, T1 4/4. The success difference is 0. With 16 distinct walks (n < 20) it is NOT resolved; this design has no power to separate the arms on success.

WALKED YAW (deg, computed from the terminal base quaternion; '*' = in-sample tuning seed), sorted: s3 0.73, s5 0.90, s7 1.48, s4 1.53, s0 1.96, s1* 1.96, s15 2.31, s2* 2.40, s12 2.85, s13 3.10, s9 3.85, s10 4.52, s14 5.05, s11 6.16, s8 7.25, s6 7.78.
- 16 walks: median 2.63, IQR 1.74–4.79, max 7.78. Counts: >=5 deg 4, >=7 deg 2, >=10 deg 0. Minimum margin to 10 deg is 2.22 deg.
- 14 out-of-sample walks: median 2.98, max 7.78, 0 >= 10 deg.
- Heading-failure rate p(yaw >= 10), exact Clopper-Pearson two-sided 95% upper bound: 0/16 -> 20.6%; 0/14 out-of-sample -> 23.2%.
- p(yaw >= 7) = 2/16 = 12.5%, CP 95% [1.6%, 38.3%].
- T1 duplicates: s0 1.96 and s1 1.96, identical to their T0 walks.
- For reference, the runner's 3-D base_orientation_error (roll+pitch+yaw) maxes at 8.34 deg (s6). Still < 10.

WALKED XY (cm), by seed: s0 0.85, s1 0.83, s2 1.26, s3 1.22, s4 2.39, s5 0.22, s6 3.50, s7 0.80, s8 1.04, s9 0.84, s10 1.88, s11 0.79, s12 1.69, s13 0.88, s14 0.74, s15 0.60. Median 0.87 cm. Two walks exceed 2 cm (s4 2.39, s6 3.50). One exceeds 3 cm (s6), so rule 2(e) fires. Rate of XY > 3 cm: 1/16, CP 95% [0.16%, 30.2%]. Seed 6 is the worst walk on both yaw and XY (dy = -3.48 cm lateral). Seed 8 has high yaw (7.25) with low XY (1.04), so the two errors are not strictly coupled.

DETERMINISM CHECK: PASS, bitwise. T0-dev-01 s0 (affd6f8 / ws f1b7), T1-dev-01 s0 and T1-dev-02 s0 (1badfd9 / ws 0231) give an identical terminal base pose and mean reward (-26.2488). The same holds for seed 1 (reward -27.2704). Analyst re-render of R-053 s6 at the current HEAD reproduces the terminal pose exactly.

PAIRED BLOCK-POSITION ERROR, v2_walk - v0, per (scene, seed); T1 uses the per-episode mean over 2 blocks; R-076 values are rounded to 0.1 cm in RUN.md. Over 20 pairs: median -0.11 cm, range -0.18 to +0.05, 18/20 negative (sign test p ~ 4e-4). This is far inside the +0.3 cm margin. Walking does not degrade placement.
- Split (iii): R-076 pairs (s0-3 + T1, n = 8) median -0.09; 1badfd9 pairs (s4-15, n = 12) median -0.13. Same sign, no reuse flag.
- Note: H-014 planned to REUSE R-003/R-005 for those 8 pairs. The runner instead ran a fresh v0 (R-076), which is a stricter control but on a third commit.
- Seed 6 placed at 2.35 cm vs v0 2.29 despite the 3.5 cm base offset. The scripted arm re-targets in world frame via sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge, so the base error is absorbed. The XY > 3 cm refutation is therefore a mechanism/spec refutation, not observed task harm.

PROVENANCE. Commits and workspace sha256 (the sha covers lab/**, methods/**, prompts/**, including lab/sumo_tasks/spot_walk_approach.py, lab/stages/sumo_build.py, lab/sumo_adapter/run_task.py, methods/v2_walk.yaml):
- R-047..R-051 (T0 s0-4): affd6f8, ws f1b7b9ba.
- R-052..R-057 (s5-10): 1badfd9, ws f1b7b9ba.
- R-058..R-063 (s11-15 + T1): 1badfd9, ws 0231019 (a different workspace at the same HEAD = uncommitted edit, i.e. dirty for at least one state).
- Control R-064..R-075 (s4-15): 1badfd9, ws 0231019.
- Control R-076 (s0-3 + T1): 80d4b35, ws 3f9b7a5f.
So the treatment arm spans 2 commits and 2 workspace states, and the control spans 2 commits. The literal `git diff affd6f8 1badfd9 -- <walk/Sumo paths>` could NOT be executed: the analyst shell is denied (read-only workspace). Indirect evidence:
(1) The affd6f8 and 1badfd9 runs share the identical workspace sha f1b7…, so every hashed file, including all walk/Sumo paths, was byte-identical as executed.
(2) The reflog message for 1badfd9 is "Fix agent model/effort placement: executor.model…", i.e. agent config outside the hashed paths.
(3) The 0231 edit cannot be attributed to a file, but walk outputs are bitwise identical across f1b7 and 0231 (determinism check).
Conclusion: the walk code did not differ in any way that changes outputs.

CLEAN SUBSETS.
(A) Same commit 1badfd9 for both arms: T0 s5-15, n = 11 walks, all out-of-sample. Result: 11/11 [0.741, 1.00]; yaw max 7.78 (s6), 2 >= 7; XY max 3.50 (s6) -> still NOT CLEARED (2e); paired median -0.14 cm.
(B) Same commit AND same workspace for both arms (1badfd9/0231): T0 s11-15, n = 5. Result: 5/5 [0.566, 1.00]; yaw max 6.16; XY max 1.69 — but the T1 control is on another commit, and n = 5 is uninformative.
IMPACT: the provenance split strictly triggers H-014 VOID rule 1. It does not change any walk measurement (bitwise replay) and does not change the substantive verdict, because the decisive episode (s6) is in subset A.

IDEALIZATIONS STILL IN USE: oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision, sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge; v0 additionally fixed_base. Also a fixed nominal walk start (-1.5, 0) and goal (-0.15, 0) that never varies with the scene, so the result covers seed robustness only, not start-pose robustness.
  - failures: No task failures: 0/20 treatment, 0/20 control; no 'unreachable', no timeout (max wall 17 s).

Criterion-level misses by category:
- (1) WALK POSITION SIDE EFFECT. 1/16 walks with XY > 3 cm: s6, 3.50 cm, mostly lateral (dy -3.48 cm). 1 more in (2, 3] cm: s4, 2.39. This fires H-014 rule 2(e) and refutes the 'without hurting XY' mechanism. The X-019 pre-fix XY range was 0.2-1.6 cm, so the gain raise widened the XY tail.
- (2) HEADING TAIL. Yaw is never >= 10 deg, but 2/16 are >= 7 deg (s6 7.78, s8 7.25) and 4/16 are >= 5 deg. That is the AT RISK 'clustered near 10' condition. The design prediction ('1-4 deg, max < 5') was wrong for 4/16 seeds. The post-fix smoke yaws (2.1-2.4) were not representative.
- (3) PROVENANCE. Mixed commits (affd6f8 / 1badfd9 / 80d4b35) and a mid-arm workspace change at the same HEAD (dirty tree). This fires VOID rule 1 literally. Shown non-causal for the walk by bitwise replay.

VISUAL: rendered R-053 T0-dev-01 s6 (no failures existed, so the worst walk was used). Codex on prompts/analyze_rollout/v1.md: failure_visible = false, "walk appears stable; b0 grasped, carried, released without drop/collision; final heading offset does not visibly affect placement." The re-render reproduced the logged terminal pose exactly.

Common mode in BOTH arms: final block x ~ 0.831-0.837 vs target 0.85, a systematic ~1.5-2 cm undershoot in x that dominates the ~2.2 cm placement error. It comes from the scripted arm / weld release, not the walk.
  - review: reject by reviewer: REJECT. H-012 (created 1791081214) and H-014 (1791082442) both predate the first evaluated run R-047 (1791084088), all scenes are dev scenes with final_eval=false, and the planned 16 distinct T0 walks plus 4 T1 treatment episodes are present. A fresh v0 exists for every treatment (scene, seed) pair. The pooled run recomputation is 40/40 success, Wilson 95% CI [0.9124,1.0], consistent with two 20/20 arms ([0.8389,1.0] each); therefore the success difference is zero and unresolved, as RS-015 notes. However, H-014 preregistered an ordered decision rule in which VOID precedes NOT CLEARED/refutation, and explicitly voids the test if the tree is dirty or treatment and fresh-v0 commits differ. That condition fires: treatment spans affd6f8 and 1badfd9 plus workspace states f1b7... and 0231...; controls span 1badfd9/0231... and 80d4b35/3f9b.... Exact code-state matched treatment/control pairs exist only for seeds 11-15. Identical hashed workspace contents across affd6f8/1badfd9 and bit-identical end poses are useful non-causality evidence, but cannot waive the preregistered provenance gate. Thus the seed-6 XY=3.50 cm observation is substantive evidence for a successor/diagnostic claim, but it cannot set H-012/H-014 to refuted in this void experiment; their status should return to open pending a clean, code-matched rerun. The idealizations and fixed-start limitation are adequately listed. R-064, R-075, and R-076 contain no restoration flag, and no restoration/deletion record is queryable, so the deterministically rebuilt v0 folders also lack an auditable provenance annotation. Decisive defect: RS-015 simultaneously says the strict verdict is VOID yet records refutes=[H-014,H-012] and drove both hypotheses to refuted, contradicting the preregistered rule ordering.
  - surprise: 1) The yaw distribution has a tail the designer did not predict. 11/16 walks sit at 0.7-3.9 deg, then 4.5, 5.1, 6.2, 7.3, 7.8 deg. The prediction was max < 5. The margin to the 10 deg line is only 2.2 deg, at a single fixed start pose.
2) The worst seed (6) is worst on yaw AND XY (3.5 cm lateral), yet its placement (2.35 cm) is no worse than v0's (2.29). The state bridge plus world-frame scripted arm absorb base error. The pre-registered XY > 3 cm line protects against a harm that this idealized pipeline cannot express. It will matter once scripted_arm / sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge are removed.
3) Paired block error is slightly BETTER under v2_walk (median -0.11 cm, 18/20 pairs negative), against an expected 'no worse'. This is probably a small geometric effect of the base offset on the arm's systematic x-undershoot, not a real improvement.
4) The runner's commit label hid a dirty-tree state change: same HEAD 1badfd9, workspace sha f1b7 -> 0231 between R-057 and R-058. Fingerprinting caught it; the runner should refuse to run on a sha mismatch inside one experiment.
- **RS-016** runs R-078: 0.69 [0.44, 0.86] (n=16); R-079: 0.62 [0.39, 0.82] (n=16); R-077: 0.95 [0.83, 0.99] (n=40); R-005: 1.00 [0.93, 1.00] (n=48); R-017: 0.98 [0.93, 0.99] (n=128); R-035: 0.94 [0.72, 0.99] (n=16)

Fresh off-grid is 11/16 (inside the 11-13 band), T1 is 24/24 and median |e| is <= 1.0 cm. No REFUTED clause fires. But the PARTIAL premise ('perception is fixed; residual failures are structural mis-parses or x shortfall') is CONTRADICTED by the per-block split (see failure_analysis). Rung 5 off-grid is NOT cleared.

(a) CODE VERSION. Part B is code-matched: R-078 (git 1c4abe0) and R-079 (git aa5cc95) share workspace_sha256 e00db7b6..., and .git/logs/HEAD shows 1c4abe0->aa5cc95 only changed the tracking of LAB_STATUS.md and the model-call log, not lab/ methods/ prompts/. Part A (R-077) ran on git 80d4b35, workspace 3f9b7a5f... Between Part A and Part B the commit bb3a96c 'Lab work in progress (agent changes)' landed (t=1791085159, after R-077 was recorded at 1791085043), so the tracked lab/methods/prompts content DIFFERS between Part A and Part B. I could not diff it (read-only workspace, no shell). Why it matters: (i) the confirmatory verdict mixes T1 on 3f9b7a with off-grid on e00db7. This is low risk for T1 at 24/24, but should be confirmed. (ii) The old-vs-fresh off-grid comparison (c) is CONFOUNDED by code if bb3a96c touched vlm_measure.py or closed_loop_place.py. Action for the engineer: git diff 80d4b35 1c4abe0 -- lab/ methods/ prompts/. Amendment clause (7) 'VOID if the run fingerprint changes between calls': the fingerprint id changes R-077 -> R-078 -> R-079. I read the clause as within-arm consistency, so I do not void, but the PI should rule. Every v46 episode has perceive.cached=false and cache_mode=fresh_per_seed. R-078 logs prompt, image and response hashes. R-077 logs only vlm_output_sha256, which still satisfies 'VLM reading hash'. Pre-registration holds: the amendment (t=1791084876.9) predates the R-077 fingerprint (1791084900.1). RESTORED CONTROLS: all three reused v0 folders have summary.json restored:true: R-005 (runs/1791061372_v0), R-017 (runs/1791070664_v0) and R-035 (runs/1791081039_v0). They were rebuilt by deterministic re-simulation and match the record. Also note R-035 and R-017 were originally recorded on dirty commits (911b67d+dirty).

(b) CONFIRMATORY, paired by (scene, seed), vs v0 only. Wilson 95% CIs.
- T1 (24 eps): v46 24/24 = 1.00 [0.86, 1.00]; v0 (R-005/R-017, seed 0-1 rows) 24/24 = 1.00 [0.86, 1.00]. 0 discordant. T1 >= 23/24 is met.
- Fresh off-grid Part B (16 eps): v46 11/16 = 0.69 [0.44, 0.86]; v0 (R-079) 10/16 = 0.63 [0.39, 0.82]. Paired difference +6.3 pp, approx 95% CI [-34, +47] pp (Wald, paired). Discordant pairs: 6 favor v46 (T2-dev-17 s0/s1, T3-dev-17 s0/s1, T3-dev-18 s0, T3-dev-19 s0) and 5 favor v0 (T2-dev-16 s0/s1, T2-dev-18 s0/s1, T3-dev-19 s1). Exact McNemar p = 1.00. By tier: v46 T2 4/8 [0.22, 0.78] and T3 7/8 [0.53, 0.98]; v0 T2 6/8 [0.41, 0.93] and T3 4/8 [0.22, 0.78]. The two methods fail on DIFFERENT scenes: v46 fails the bridges, v0 fails the staircases and offset columns.
- Combined confirmatory (40): v46 35/40 = 0.875 [0.74, 0.95]; v0 34/40 = 0.85 [0.71, 0.93].
- Threshold off-grid >= 14/16: NOT met (11). Threshold median |e| <= 1.0 cm: MET on inferred e (approx 0.5-0.7 cm over Part B blocks; see the caveat below). Against the 0.8 target, v46 Part B is 0.69 with a lower bound of 0.44, so the target is not met. Relative to v0, v46 is about equal (+1 episode, unresolved at n=16 per arm). 'MATCHES v0' in H-013's sense (>= v0 - 1) holds trivially because v0 itself only reaches 10/16. That says the fresh scenes are hard for placement, not that v46 is good.
- CAVEAT ON e: the episode logs do NOT record the perceived y per block, which the pre-registration required. I inferred e as pre_release_y - true_y, which is valid when the closed-loop residual pre_release_err <= ~0.5 cm, as it is for most blocks. Where the correction stalled (residual 2-2.7 cm), e is only bounded. The median |e| verdict is therefore approximate.

(c) EXPLORATORY.
- Old off-grid (Part A, mask-tuned scenes): v46 14/16 = 0.875 [0.64, 0.96] vs v0 R-035 15/16 = 0.94 [0.72, 0.99]. Paired: 1 discordant, favoring v0 (T3-dev-13 s0). T2-dev-11 s0 failed in both.
- Staircase b2 REFUTED clause: NOT triggered. T2-dev-11 b2: s0 pre-release y 3.96 cm vs true 4.0 cm (e ~ 0.0 cm; that episode failed because the white_brick base was never placed), s1 final error 0.3 cm. T3-dev-14 b2: 0.4 cm in both seeds.
- Old off-grid median |e|: < 1 cm (final block errors mostly 0.2-1.0 cm).
- Old vs fresh divergence: 14/16 vs 11/16, -19 pp, Fisher p ~ 0.4, unresolved. Qualitatively it IS the flagged pattern: on old scenes only structural parse failures (seed 0 only), on fresh scenes gross lateral mis-measurement on bridges containing a WHITE cube (T2-dev-16, T2-dev-18). That is the block colour the mask fix was tuned for. This counts as an overfitting flag for the white-mask fix, but code drift (a) is an alternative explanation until the diff is checked.
- 'Beats v45' was not evaluated here (no Part B v45 run); exploratory only anyway.

STATUS: H-013 stays OPEN with the pre-registered outcome PARTIAL. It is not supported (11 < 14) and not refuted (no REFUTED clause fires). The successor should NOT follow the PARTIAL template ('not more lateral perception'). Lateral perception on white-cube bridges is the top v46 failure.

IDEALIZATIONS STILL IN USE. v46: oracle_build_order, oracle_block_pose (the closed-loop correction reads sim truth), fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision, clean_distinct_colour_renders, x_zero, yaw_zero, z_from_layer. v0: oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, fixed_base, weld_grasp, scripted_arm, no_arm_block_collision. Dev scenes only; held-out untouched.

VIDEOS: v46 failure runs/1791085245_v46_vlm_measure/T2-dev-16_s0.gif (contact sheet rollout_T2-dev-16_s0.png). v0 failure runs/1791085380_v0/T2-dev-17_s0.gif. RUN.md pages: runs/1791085245_v46_vlm_measure/RUN.md and runs/1791085380_v0/RUN.md.
  - failures: Auto categories: v46 Part B = placement_error 4, perception_mismatch+missing_block 1. v0 Part B = placement_error 6. The per-block split (e = perceived y - true y, inferred from pre-release pose; placement = final - pre-release) re-attributes most of v46's 'placement_error' to PERCEPTION.

v46 Part B, 5 failed episodes:
1. T2-dev-16 s0 and s1 (bridge: white cube y=-7.1, purple cube y=+6.2, blue brick y=+1.7 cm): GROSS LATERAL PERCEPTION. Both supports were perceived at y ~ 0 (pre-release +0.1/+0.2 cm, residual 0.4 cm), so e = +7.2 and -5.8 cm. The brick was perceived at +8.7 cm, so e = +7.0 cm. The two cubes were placed into the same spot, the purple cube was shoved 42-50 cm away, and the brick tipped 44 deg. Placement itself was accurate to its (wrong) target. Both seeds returned the IDENTICAL VLM response hash (e1393af5...) with the same image, so the error is deterministic in the measure step, not VLM sampling. Category: perception (lateral measure), mislabeled placement_error.
2. T2-dev-18 s0 and s1 (bridge: purple y=-5.8, WHITE cube y=+7.6, green brick y=-0.9 cm): MIXED. Purple e ~ -0.5 to -0.8 cm. Brick e ~ -1.0 cm (placed OK, 0.9 cm final). The white cube's closed-loop correction STALLED: pre_release_err 2.6 cm after 2 passes, x ~1.7-1.9 cm short, z 0.6 cm low, final 3.7-3.8 cm. Its perceived y is only bounded to [2.8, 6.6] cm, so e is between -1.0 and -4.8 cm. Category: placement (x shortfall / stalled correction, H-006) plus likely white-cube perception error. Same white-cube involvement as #1.
3. T3-dev-19 s1 (pyramid): STRUCTURAL PARSE. The top yellow brick was mismatched or missing from the VLM structure, never placed (pre_release null), final 52 cm. The other lateral e values were small (|e| <= 1.1 cm; red b0 correction stalled at 2.7 cm but settled at 1.5 cm). Category: perception (structure).
Net: 5/5 failures involve perception (2 lateral-gross, 1 structural, 2 mixed), 0 pure placement. Lateral-e tail on successes: T3-dev-20 s0 red cube e ~ -2.1 cm (final 2.0 cm, passed). T2-dev-15 s1 base cube stalled correction (2.2 cm residual, final 2.4 cm, passed). The pre-registered SUPPORTED condition 'every failing block |e| <= 1.5 cm' fails badly (6-7 cm).

v0 Part B, 6 failed episodes (oracle spec, so e = 0 by construction; all are placement):
1. T2-dev-17 s0 and s1 (staircase 0/-2.4/-4.7): the open-loop arm's pick-side lateral bias put b0 at +1.6 cm and b1 at -3.8 cm (plus a 1.4 cm x shortfall), so b1 overhung b0 by 5.4 cm vs 2.4 cm designed. b1 rolled 90 deg and b2 dropped onto it. Codex on the contact sheet agrees: misalignment visible before release, phase = place.

This justifies refuting H-014 and amended H-012's 'without hurting XY' mechanism, but not claiming a task-success harm or a population failure rate. The result properly lists the treatment idealizations and fixed nominal start/goal limitation (with fixed_base additionally identified for v0). Separately, the claim that rung 2 meets the T0 success target is justified: 16/16 with Wilson lower bound 0.806 (rounded 0.81) exceeds 0.80, independent of mechanism clause 2(e).

- **RS-018** runs R-082: 0.00 [0.00, 0.14] (n=24); R-083: 1.00 [0.86, 1.00] (n=24)
  - interpretation: X-043 / H-011 (rung 4, v4_mpc vs fresh v0, T0-dev-01..06 x seeds 0-3, 24 pairs). The two runs are comparable: R-082 and R-083 share git c733d69, fingerprint 7f6eaa11, workspace_sha256 94b8f00f, the same 45-entry workspace_files list in the same order, and the same two Sumo native .so sha256s. The (scene, seed) pairs are identical.
RESULT: v4_mpc 0/24 = 0.00 (Wilson 95% CI [0.00, 0.14]) vs v0 24/24 = 1.00 [0.86, 1.00]. The success difference is -100 pp (Newcombe 95% CI about [-100, -79] pp). Median block position error is 45.0 cm vs 2.36 cm (difference of medians +42.6 cm); median angle error is 4.3 vs 2.1 deg. Every v4_mpc episode is above 3 cm (min 4.1 cm, max 63.0 cm). With n=24 per arm and zero overlap, this gap is resolved.
PRE-REGISTERED RULE, applied in order:
(1) VOID: no. bridge_snaps_to_goal=false. The lab block is teleported to the Sumo terminal free-joint pose (handoff 0.0 cm / 0.0 deg; post-transfer drift ~0.007 cm). The goal is set per scene from the spec (scene_object_goal_pose), and the Sumo echo matched within 1e-9, so there are no sumo_object_goal_mismatch failures. All six T0 scenes happen to share the goal [0.85, 0, 0.05].
(2) NOT CLEARED: yes. There are 24/24 failures and 24/24 blocks > 3 cm. Stop here; AT RISK, CLEARED and IMPROVES are not evaluated.
=> H-011 is NOT CLEARED, so H-011 is refuted.
TIMING: each episode ran 201/201 MPC steps at max_opt_iters_per_step=1. Planning took about 12.4-13.5 s per episode (wall 13-14 s) against the 600 s timeout, so this is not a compute-budget or timeout failure.
DIAGNOSIS (4 rollouts inspected: Sumo episode logs plus codex on the contact sheets):
(a) This is not a placement: the Sumo task (SpotBoxPush subclass) never grasps. The block moves only when the arm or body pushes it, and it never lifts (terminal z 0.04996 in every inspected episode).
(b) T0-dev-04 s2: the block is untouched; terminal equals start to 1e-13 m. The base walked from (-0.03, 0.96) to (0.74, 0.78) and yawed about 60 deg off its approach heading. codex: 'gripper never contacts'.
(c) T0-dev-01 s0: the block moved 0.26 cm (incidental). The base walked to (0.77, -0.93), 113.6 deg off heading, and never reached the block. codex: 'never visibly contacts'.
(d) T0-dev-03 s0 (best case, 4.1 cm): the block was pushed 0.42 m and overshot to (0.882, -0.026) at 5.6 deg. The base ended at x=1.52, past the goal. codex: 'pushed by arm/gripper, overshoots'.
(e) T0-dev-05 s2 (worst, 63 cm): the block was pushed the wrong way, from (0.424, 0.341) to (0.393, 0.433), and spun 52 deg.
CEM COST TRAJECTORY: not available. run_task.py logs only per-episode mean_reward, with no per-step cost. Mean reward was -117 (untouched), -124 (untouched), -128 (pushed away) and -61 (near miss). An untouched block at about 0.47 m contributes a constant 220*0.47 = 103 to the placement term, so the reward is dominated by a term that stays flat until contact.
LIKELY ROOT CAUSE (from code reading; not tested by ablation): the cost gives CEM almost no gradient toward contact. reset_pose puts Spot 0.8 m behind the block on the start->goal line, so the gripper must travel about 0.8 m before the 220/m placement term changes at all. The only approach shaping is w_gripper_object = 6/m (37x weaker than placement) plus gripper height. With 24 samples, 1 CEM iteration per control step and a 2 s horizon, most sampled rollouts never touch the block. The planner then drifts or wanders (base yaw errors of 60-115 deg) or pushes off-axis. When it does make contact, nothing in the cost stops the push at the goal, which explains the overshoot. Scene geometry is a further factor: T0 starts are about 0.43-0.55 m from the goal at about ±35 deg off the x axis, while the task's default smoke geometry is an on-axis push along the robot's +x.
THE 0.87 cm SMOKE: X-043 feasibility cites 'smoke 0.87 cm', and H-011 cites artifact sumo-1791077387-spot_block_mpc_place, which is not a lab record. Its timestamp 1791077387 falls between commit 911b67d (1791062260) and 5c3f808 (1791082914, 'agent-built stages, Sumo tasks'). It therefore ran on an uncommitted working tree that no commit pins, so its task cost, CEM budget, v4_mpc.yaml and bridge cannot be recovered or diffed against c733d69 from the record. Two facts in the current code weaken it as evidence. (i) The direct run_task path uses the task defaults (block start (-0.45, 0), on-axis), not the pipeline's per-scene start/goal/size/mass, and the bridge goal-echo guard did not exist on that path. (ii) The v4_mpc.yaml comment states that a 6 s smoke 'covered only 0.262 m' of the 0.472 m T0-dev-01 move. This contradicts a 0.87 cm placement on T0-dev-01 geometry. A probable explanation is that the 0.87 cm figure came from a different start/goal (or from the success tolerance applied at a different geometry), not from the T0 scenes. This is unverified: with read-only access I could not run git diff, and the artifact file was not found.
IDEALIZATIONS STILL IN USE: oracle_structure_spec, oracle_build_order, oracle_block_pose (Sumo reads the true block state), sumo_to_assembly_state_bridge (teleport), a public Sumo box proxy for block dynamics, and a lab-world fixed_base (the Sumo base actually walks; its pose is not transferred). LISTED BUT NOT IN EFFECT: 'weld_grasp' and 'no_arm_block_collision'. In Sumo the arm collides with the block and nothing is welded; this is why the method pushes.

- Zero bridge, handoff, settle-drift, goal-mismatch, fall, or timeout failures.

A fresh baseline is present: R-083 v0 uses exactly the same six T0-dev scenes, seeds 0-3, commit/workspace fingerprint 7f6eaa11, and 24 (scene,seed) pairs as R-082. Both runs are final_eval=false and list only dev scenes, so no held-out scene was used. The run summaries and RS-018 agree: R-082 is 0/24 with Wilson 95% CI [0.000,0.138], R-083 is 24/24 with [0.862,1.000], failures are 24 mpc_place_failed versus none, and recomputing both runs pools to 24/48 overall as expected. The arm-level intervals do not overlap, all 24 pairs are discordant in the same direction, and H-011's preregistered any-failure NOT CLEARED rule is decisively triggered for the implementation that ran. H-011 was created before R-082, so the outcome rule is preregistered rather than post-hoc. However, construct validity is decisive: H-011 describes/lists weld_grasp and no_arm_block_collision, while R-082's Sumo task has neither a weld grasp nor disabled arm-block collision; it performs non-prehensile pushing and never lifts the block. Those are not residual idealizations but absent defining mechanics. Therefore the run did not test the method as described by H-011, and H-011 should be 'not cleared / method-as-described untested,' not 'refuted.' RS-018 appropriately discloses the mismatch and distinguishes listed-but-not-in-effect idealizations, but its final inference still overclaims. The cited 0.87 cm smoke does not rescue validity: it ran on an uncommitted, unrecoverable tree with default/on-axis geometry and cannot be tied to the frozen per-scene treatment; it is at most weak feasibility evidence and does not invalidate the current run's measured failures. The proposed cause (approach shaping 6/m versus placement 220/m, 24 CEM samples, one iteration) is appropriately labeled in RS-018 as a likely root cause from code reading and explicitly 'not tested by ablation'; it must remain a hypothesis, not a demonstrated causal result. Scope must also remain these six dev scenes—the 24 episodes are four seeds per scene, not evidence for broad scene-population or real-world performance.
